The mount is the unit `zsc unit create sshfs-<alias>` makes (`zerops@sshfs-<alias>.service`), which runs sshfs in the foreground and restarts it when it exits, so a `fusermount` alone is undone within seconds (live-verified 2026-10-24): an unmount removes the unit first and runs `fusermount` only while the mount is still there, a unit it cannot remove fails the unmount before any `fusermount` — a forced unmount of a stale mount too, which ignores a failed removal only once no unit is left, as an orphan mount may have none — every unit removal clears the instance's failed state with `reset-failed` (live 2026-10-24: `zsc unit remove` leaves `zerops@sshfs-<alias>.service` listed failed), a failed instance left after the unit file is gone counts as a unit left by that removal — only there: `zerops_mount action=status` and an unmount of an unmounted service ask for the unit file alone, so a failed instance an older zcp left on every unmount is never "pending" or `UNIT_CLEANED` — and a failed unmount's advice names `sudo zsc unit remove sshfs-<alias>` before `fusermount -uz`. `TestValidateLeaseAlias`, `TestLeaseAliasStanza_Rejects*`, `TestLeaseAliasInstall_StagesThenRenamesIntoPlace`, `TestLeaseAliasInstall_RenameFails_SaysSoAndRemovesStaging`, `TestUnmount_GoneOnceTheUnitStops_NoFusermount`, `TestUnmount_UnitRemoveFails_NoFusermountThatTheUnitWouldUndo`, `TestHasUnit_AFailedInstanceIsNoUnit`, `TestCleanupUnit_RemoveFailsButNothingIsLeft_Succeeds`, `TestDetachMount_FailedUnmount_NamesTheUnitNotFusermountAlone`, `TestForceUnmount_UnitRemoveFails_FailsWhileTheUnitStays`. |
| DP-2 | The orchestrator is the truth for a lease; zcp's record `.zcp/state/leases/<alias>.json` (mode 0600) only carries the lease string and what zcp needs to find its own leases — never a ServiceMeta, and no reservation time. **Whose a container is** is asked of the orchestrator with the record's own lease, and nothing else decides it: `POST /container/{id}/renew`, the lease only in `X-Swarm-Lease` (DP-3). 200 with `lockState` `reserved` (or `stopping`/`starting`) is the lease's own — running or stopped, however its `lockSince` moved; 200 `deleting`, 400 `unknown-lease`, and 400 `container-not-found` — also the reasonless `containerAction` "Container not found" the orchestrator passes on as a 400 right after a delete, which the client maps to `container-not-found` (only that 400: the same words with any other status stay unverified) — are `lost`; any other answer (none within the read budget, a 5xx, a 401/403, a pool zcp cannot reach) is `unverified`. A lease refused `unknown-lease` is told apart by a read of its container by id: reserved is another holder's, otherwise free. A lease of a pool that is gone, re-created or being deleted is `lost` without a renew (DP-20). zcp asks again right before every call that changes a container or reaches it — a release, delete, stop or start, the guard's ssh, a sync's or a rewire's ssh, and every tool that reaches a lease's container over ssh or sshfs: `zerops_dev_server` (every action), `zerops_verify`, `zerops_deploy` and `zerops_deploy_batch` from a lease, `zerops_mount action=mount`, and `zcp init`'s lease restore before it remounts a lease at boot (`ops.LeaseTargetChecked`: one renew per call, the parked refusal of DP-28 after it; `zcp init` asks every lease whose record is not parked at once, within 15 s, through a check the command layer builds over the API client serve mode builds — only when there is a lease to mount — and a lost lease's line names the release that drops its record, an unverified one's — no answer, no orchestrator, no API client — names why and `zerops_mount action=mount serviceHostname=<alias>`, which asks again; the aliases are rendered as before) — and acts only on the lease's own: a `lost` lease is never read or written over ssh and its container never reset, deleted, stopped or started; an `unverified` one — or any lease, for a zcp that has no orchestrator to ask — is read from and sent nothing, and every refusal of it names `zerops_lease action=status`, which asks again; a lost one's refusal names the release that drops its record. The plan never names an `unverified` lease as a stage's source (live recheck 2026-10-24: `zerops_dev_server` on a lease another holder had taken ssh'd into its container and started `zcp-devsup` there, and `zerops_verify` called it healthy). The orchestrator's `held-by-other` answer to a release carrying the lease is final; to a delete, stop or start it drops the record only once a renew of the lease agrees the lease ended; while the renew still gives the container to the lease the call is refused as any other orchestrator failure, and while the renew does not answer the container may be another holder's — nothing more is sent to it, mounted or written, and the refusal names the status that asks again. A drop takes the record as `lost` with its alias stanza and mount and is written — inside a refused branch release (DP-23) and a re-park (DP-28) too — never "may succeed in a moment" (live 4g: a lease another holder took read `held`, its release kept refusing over ssh, its record could not be dropped). **A record is dropped only while it is still the lease's own**: zcp reads `<alias>.json` again and removes it, unmounts `/var/www/<alias>` and re-renders the aliases only when it still holds the dropped lease and container — checked before any unmount, a release's or a park's, so another lease's mount is never taken down. The read and the removal hold the alias's record lock (`<stateDir>/locks/record-<alias>.lock`, a flock waited for at most 10 s), which every write of a record holds too, so another process's write never lands between them; and a record is written again — by a park, an unpark, a re-park, an acquire after its checkout — only while it still names the lease and its container, checked and written under the same lock: one that names another lease by then, or is gone or unreadable, is left alone, the orchestrator is asked again and the lease dropped as `lost`, or the call refused. Another zcp sharing the state directory may have released the container and taken it again under the same alias meanwhile — a released container stays as the pool's warm one (DP-27), so the next acquire takes it within seconds — and that lease's record, mount and stanza are left alone, the view saying the alias names another lease now; a release then hands the branch's other leases no overlay without the alias, which may still name a lease of the branch (DP-26). A record zcp cannot read is kept as well, the view saying why and how to clear it. Two limits stay by design: a renew and the call it vouches for are two requests — the orchestrator offers no call conditional on a lease — so a lease that ends between them is acted on once more; and a zcp older than the record and group locks, sharing the state directory, takes neither. Live-verified 2026-10-24: a renew with the holder's own lease answers 200 in ~36 ms and changes nothing (`lockSince`, `dirty`, status, processes, files), never starts a stopped container, and is accepted after the holder's own stop and start; with a wrong lease, or any lease on a free container, it answers 400 `unknown-lease`, not retryable; a lease taken with TTL 0 — every lease zcp takes — never expires, so it ends only by a release, a reset or a delete; after a delete it answers 200 `deleting` for ~10–24 s, then 400 "Container not found" with `metadata: null`, then — once a `GET /container` has run — 400 `container-not-found`; `lockSince` restarts at every stop and start (an acquire's, stop's and start's answer carries it; a delete answers an empty body, a reset release none), so no recorded time tells the lease's own reservation from another's; a release of a free container checks no lease and resets it, answering `released` (live 4f); a release with a wrong lease on a reserved container answers 400 `containerAction`, reason `held-by-other`, retryable `true` (live 4g), while a renew of the same answers `unknown-lease` — the reason vocabulary is per endpoint. On a delete, stop or start `held-by-other` is not live-verified, nor what the orchestrator answers its own holder while it stops or starts the container — hence the confirming renew. Status renews every record at once (each within the read budget) and reconciles: a record whose lease is lost is `lost`, its note saying why (gone, free, another holder's, being deleted, the pool re-created); a reserved container with no record — or one its holder is stopping or starting under its reservation — is `unknown-holder`; a record without its alias or mount is `unmounted`; a record whose renew answers its container stopped and reserved is `parked`, checked before the mount (DP-28); a record whose renew is not answered keeps the listing's state, `unverified`, with why (DP-11); a pool whose orchestrator does not list its containers still loses what zcp knows without it — every record of a pool being deleted by its search row or, after an orchestrator that could not be reached, gone or being deleted by its read by id, and a record taken before the pool was re-created — reported `lost` with why, never `unverified`, in a status of that pool or of every pool — in `action=status` and in the envelope's `leases[]` alike, whose reader checks the same host side (inside a container the system mounter and the alias file; `docs/spec-mate.md` §2.8a.3) — and `zcp init` — the boot step a zcp@1 container runs — re-renders the aliases from the records after a zcp redeploy and remounts each lease its orchestrator vouches for, as a step that never fails the boot. Whether a name is a lease is decided by zcp's record of it, never by its shape: `zerops_mount action=mount` mounts a name zcp holds a lease record of as that lease (`ops.MountLease`, no service lookup) and looks any other name up as a service — one shaped like an alias included — refusing it `SERVICE_NOT_FOUND` before sshfs runs when no service has it (`TestMountTool_AliasShapedName_MountedOnlyWithALeaseRecord`, `TestMountService_AliasShapedName_LookedUpLikeAnyName`). `zerops_mount action=status` reports a lease's mount — mounted, stale or pending — without writing a probe file through it: its sshfs reconnects to the container's hostname, which the pool hands to its next holder once the lease ended, and a status asks no orchestrator. `TestLeaseList_*`, `TestInitSSHFS_RemountsLeases`, `TestRun_Container_RestoresLeases`, `TestRun_Container_LeaseRestoreFailure_DegradesNotFatal`, `TestLeaseTool_UnmountedLease_EnvelopeSaysWhatStatusSays`, `TestEnvelopeLeaseHost_ChecksTheHostSideInsideAContainerOnly`, `TestLease_Integration_UnmountedLease_EnvelopeSaysWhatStatusSays`, `TestSwarmClient_Renew_ReadsTheLiveAnswers`, `TestSwarmClient_HeldByOther_EveryLeaseCallReadsTheLiveRefusal`, `TestLeaseOwnership_Status_RenewDecides`, `TestLeaseOwnership_StopStartOutsideZcp_StaysOurs`, `TestLeaseOwnership_LostAnswerThenRefusal_StaysOurs`, `TestLeaseOwnership_ConcurrentParkDuringRelease_NeverDropsALiveLease`, `TestLeaseOwnership_HeldByOther_DropsTheRecord`, `TestLeaseOwnership_HeldByOtherForItsOwnLease_KeptUnlessTheRenewAgrees`, `TestLeaseOwnership_AliasReused_AnotherLeasesRecordLeftAlone`, `TestLeaseOwnership_UnreadableRecordAtTheAlias_KeptAndSaid`, `TestLeaseOwnership_StopHeldByOther_RenewUnanswered_NothingMountedOrWritten`, `TestLeaseTool_Ownership_LeaseAnotherHolderTook_LostThenDropped`, `TestLeaseTool_Ownership_StoppedOutsideZcp_StaysParked`, `TestLeaseTool_Ownership_RenewFails_UnverifiedNeverRead`, `TestLease_Integration_LeaseReleasedOutsideAndRetaken_DroppedAsLost`, `TestLease_Integration_LeaseStoppedOutsideZcp_StaysOurs`, `TestLeaseTargetChecked_TheOrchestratorVouchesOnce`, `TestInitSSHFS_LeaseRestore_AsksItsOrchestratorFirst`, `TestInitSSHFS_NoLeaseCheck_NothingMountedNotFatal`, `TestInitSSHFS_LeaseChecks_AskedAtOnce`, `TestInitSSHFS_LeaseCheckHangs_Bounded`, `TestRun_Container_LeaseRestoreAsksFirst`, `TestInitLeaseCheck_NoAPIClient_UnverifiedSaysWhy`, `TestInitLeaseCheck_BuildsTheClientOnlyWhenAsked`, `TestInitLeaseCheck_AsksThroughLeaseTargetChecked`, `TestLease_Integration_LeaseNotOurs_ToolsNeverReachIt`, `TestMountTool_LeaseNoOneVouchesFor_NeverMounted`, `TestDeployBatch_LeaseSourceNoOrchestratorVouchesFor_Refused`, `TestMountStatus_LeaseMount_NeverWriteProbed`, `TestLeaseStatus_UnknownHolderParkingItsContainer_Named`. |
| DP-3 | Every acquire is `{start:true, clean:true, leaseTtlSeconds:0}`, run to its own deadline whatever its caller does (an acquire has no idempotency key, so one cut short would leave a container reserved for good). The orchestrator's bearer is the pool's `API_TOKEN`, read from the container's live env as `<pool>_API_TOKEN`, then as `<pool>_LEASE_TOKEN`, then through the env API. A value that is still the text of a reference (`${...}`) is never a bearer: a service variable named like the reference it holds (`<pool>_API_TOKEN=${<pool>_API_TOKEN}`) stays literal under every envIsolation, and while it exists turns every other reference to that name on the service literal too, while a different-name one resolves into the live env within ~15 s (live-verified 2026-10-23). With no bearer the refusal names any unresolved value, says to delete a same-name variable, and to add `<pool>_LEASE_TOKEN=${<pool>_API_TOKEN}` on the zcp service — never the same name (`TestLeaseAcquire_PoolBearer_LiveEnvSources`, `TestLeaseAcquire_NoUsableBearer_SaysAddADifferentNameReference`). The lease string travels only in the `X-Swarm-Lease` header — never in a URL (a transport error prints the URL), a log, a tool result, the envelope or telemetry. An orchestrator refusal reaches the agent with everything it says in words, scrubbed of the bearer and the lease: its message — left out when it is the platform's placeholder `See metadata`, which points at `meta[]` — the platform's cause (`meta[].metadata.message`), and the rest of `meta[]`: each item's code and error and every other metadata key, whatever its JSON type (live, 2026-10-25: a release of a container that could start no process answered `See metadata` alone, and the agent learned nothing of why; `TestSwarmClient_ErrorEnvelope_SaysWhatItsMetadataSays`, `TestLeaseRelease_OrchestratorRefusal_SaysWhatItsMetadataSays`). `TestSwarmClient_LeaseOnlyInHeader`, `TestLeaseTool_ResultNeverCarriesLease`, `TestLeaseAcquire_MaskedToken_UsesTheContainersLiveEnv`, `TestLeaseAcquire_CallerGoneMidAcquire_NoLeak`. |
| DP-4 | Release refuses while the container holds commits on no remote branch or uncommitted/untracked files, naming them; `force=true` skips that refusal. A reset takes every commit in `/var/www`, so the commits on no remote branch are counted over every local branch and HEAD — a branch HEAD is not on and a detached HEAD included; an unborn HEAD (no commit yet, an orphan branch) names no revision while the branches still count, so a checkout with no commit counts 0 — never HEAD's alone (`TestLeaseReleaseGuard_CommitsOnAnyLocalBranch_Refused`, `TestLeaseReleaseGuard_NoCommitYet_Passes`). A zcp with no ssh to the container (local mode) cannot check it, so an unforced release — a lease's or a branch's — is refused `PREREQUISITE_MISSING`, naming `force=true`, and releases nothing (`TestLeaseRelease_NoSSH_RefusedUnlessForced`, `TestLeaseReleaseGroup_NoSSH_RefusedUnlessForced`). Every release resets the container (`reset=true`), or removes it with its own lease when the pool already has its warm container or the container is outdated (DP-27): either way its processes end, so a dev server left on a preview port stops answering, and the next acquire starts clean; a container that cannot be reset is deleted instead (DP-15). Before any of that a release asks whose the container is (DP-2): one that is no longer the lease's — gone, free, another holder's, being deleted — is dropped as `lost` with its alias stanza and mount, its note saying why, with or without `force`: the guard never runs over ssh (it would read whoever holds the container now, or a reset image: live 4d, "not a git repository"), and the orchestrator's release and delete are never called (live 4f: a forced release reset the pool's free container under a racing acquire and answered `released`); the branch's other leases are rewired without it first, as before any release (DP-26). One the orchestrator does not answer about (`unverified`) is refused, `force` or not — nothing read, nothing changed — naming the status that asks again. The reset release asks again right before it is sent: a `lost` answer then, or a `held-by-other` answer to the release, drops the record unreleased. A container ssh cannot reach is asked about again: no longer the lease's, it is dropped unchecked as `lost` and nothing is released. An orchestrator that does not answer while its pool is still there refuses the release, `force` or not (DP-20). zcp never commits on the agent's behalf (GF-2). The check only reads: every git call it makes runs with `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0`, since a plain `git status` refreshes the checkout's `.git/index` and writes it back — a new inode and mtime, same bytes (live-verified 2026-10-23). `TestLeaseOwnership_Release_NotOurs_DropsTheRecordAndTouchesNothing`, `TestLeaseOwnership_Release_Unverified_Refused`, `TestLeaseOwnership_ConcurrentParkDuringRelease_NeverDropsALiveLease`, `TestLeaseTool_Ownership_ForcedReleaseOfAFreeContainer_NeverResetsIt`, `TestLeaseRelease_Unreachable_Lost`, `TestLeaseRelease_UnreachableOrchestratorSilent_Refused`, `TestLeaseRelease_Unpushed_Refused`, `TestLeaseRelease_Force_Resets`, `TestLeaseRelease_ResetPassedAndRecordRemoved`, `TestLeaseReleaseGuard_ReadsTheCheckoutWithoutRewritingItsIndex`. |
| DP-5 | `zerops_lease` — registered in a container only, since its orchestrators answer only inside the project (DP-3); a local zcp's tool list carries no `zerops_lease` (`TestServer_LeaseToolGating`): `action=acquire` (`pool` — or `pools`, several, one lease each, DP-17; never both — `repo` — the https clone URL, cloned as given and remembered per pool as given after the first acquire (DP-18) or seeded with a migrating dev's origin by pool-migrate's env step (§13 PM-4) — optional `branch`, and optional `from`, the branch a new one is cut from, DP-6), `action=status` (optional `pool`), `action=release` (`lease` = the alias, or `branch` — every lease of it, DP-23 — with optional `repo`; never both; optional `force`), `action=rollout` (`pool`, DP-7), `action=sync` (`lease` alone, DP-24), `action=park` and `action=unpark` (`lease` alone, DP-28); `pools` on any other action is refused. Results name the alias, the pool, the container hostname, the branch, the state and `acquiredAt` (every view of a lease zcp has a record of: acquire, status, release); status also carries each held lease's `activity` and `unsaved` (DP-21), `idle` on a lease nothing has happened in for 24 h, and `nextActions` naming the idle ones (DP-25), and each pool's `warm` (DP-27); an acquire that returned the lease zcp already held for the repository, branch and pool carries `reused: true` (DP-17), and each lease an acquire took carries `devServers` and `siblingEnv` (DP-30); an acquire or release that changed a branch's group carries `rewired`, and a status view of a lease wired to a container no lease of its group holds `wiring: "stale"` (DP-26). When the orchestrator does not answer (outside the project), the result says it answers only inside the project. `TestLeaseTool_*`, `TestLeaseViews_CarryAcquiredAt`. |
| DP-6 | Acquire puts the branch in the container's `/var/www` — empty, or holding the clone zcp started while the container was warm (DP-32), which the checkout settles first — by running git inside the container with the pool's own service env (its `GIT_TOKEN` wired the way a dev service's is); a credential never appears in a clone URL, `.git/config` or a process argument. For an https origin the repository keeps a credential helper scoped to the origin's host — and its port when that is not 443, since git matches a scope's port exactly (live, git 2.50.1: a helper under `https://host` is never asked for `https://host:8443`) — that answers from the session's `GIT_TOKEN` (pool service env reaches ssh sessions in its containers — live-verified) and answers nothing without it, so the agent's own push over the alias authenticates with nothing stored. Only https origins are taken. A branch that does not exist yet on origin is cut from `main` without tracking it: from its group's base commit when a lease of the same repository and branch was cut before (DP-17), else from the current `origin/main` — or, with `from=<branch>` (one lease or a group), from the current `origin/<from>`, every new member of the group from the one commit the first was cut from. A `from` origin does not have fails the checkout naming it, whatever the branch, and the container goes back (`INVALID_PARAMETER`): nothing is cut from another commit instead. `from` never rewrites a branch: one origin has is tracked as it is there, one its group cut before starts from the group's base, and one a lease already holds is returned as it is — each lease saying `from` was not applied. `from` names a branch a lease takes, never the branch itself, never with `main` (no acquire cuts it), and only on an acquire, each refused before a container is taken (live, 2026-10-25: an agent made two branches off an open change's branch by a raw `git push origin origin/pool-previews:refs/heads/<new>` over ssh, since an acquire would have cut them from main; `TestLeaseTool_Acquire_From_CutsTheNewBranchFromTheBase`, `TestLeaseTool_Acquire_From_BaseNotOnOrigin_Refused`, `TestLeaseTool_Acquire_From_ExistingBranch_NeverRewritten`, `TestLeaseTool_Acquire_From_BadInput_Refused`). The checkout answers the commit it cut from, and the record keeps it as `base` (a branch tracked from origin keeps none, and so does an older record — its group's next lease is cut from the current `origin/main`). The checkout also answers, on a line of its own, the container's stable number (`ZEROPS_StableNumber` of its ssh session), which the record keeps as `stableNumber` — written before the acquire lets its keys go and before any rewire — for its siblings' preview URLs (DP-31); a session without one answers none and the record keeps none (`TestLeaseCheckout_AnswersTheContainersStableNumber`). A base origin no longer has (main rewritten since) fails the checkout naming it, never cutting the branch from another commit. `TestLeaseCheckout_*`, `TestLeaseCheckout_NewBranch_CutFromTheGroupsBase`, `TestLeaseCheckout_BaseNotOnOrigin_FailsNamingIt`, `TestLeaseCheckoutCommand_PersistsHostScopedHelper`, `TestLeaseCredentialHelper_AnswersFromSessionEnvOnly`, `TestLeaseCredentialHelper_OriginOnItsOwnPort_AnswersThere` (a dev service's checkout scopes by the same rule: `TestPersistedCredentialHelper_ByShellAndHost`). A `zerops_deploy strategy=git-push` from a lease is refused: it would push the target's tracked branch, while a lease pushes its own (`TestDeployTool_LeaseSource_GitPushRefused`). The checkout leaves the repository commit-ready: a fresh pool container carries no git identity (live 2026-10-24: the agent's first commit failed "Author identity unknown"), so zcp fills the robot identity set-if-absent after the checkout, the one fragment every commit-ready site uses (`TestLeaseCheckoutCommand_SetsACommitIdentityOnlyWhenAbsent`). |
| DP-7 | A deploy whose target is a pool changes only containers the pool creates after it, whatever its source (a lease, a dev service, a local checkout): existing containers, leased or free, keep their version (`current:false`). Its result says so (`reachesNewContainersOnly`), runs no verify or public-access step against the pool, and points at `zerops_lease action=rollout`. `action=rollout pool=<pool>` calls the orchestrator's `POST /container/rollout` without `force`: free outdated containers are replaced within the pool's minimum and maximum; reserved ones (every lease, zcp's included) are skipped as `held-by-other` and keep their version until released — a release removes an outdated container with its own lease (DP-27), and the orchestrator does not replace a held outdated container its holder deleted (live-verified 2026-10-23), so the old version leaves the pool at release and the pool's top-up creates a current one — zcp never forces a rollout (force needs the admin token, and would take a lease from its holder); outdated containers with work in progress retire by themselves. The result counts replaced, created, retiring and skipped containers with each skip's reason; a pool whose minimum equals its maximum is refused (`pool-fixed-size`) and the result says a rollout needs room between them. `TestSwarmClient_Rollout*`, `TestLeaseRollout_*`, `TestDeployTool_ToPool_SaysOnlyNewContainers`. |
| DP-8 | A deploy from a lease — single or batch — reads it only once its orchestrator vouches for it (DP-2). A stage tracks main (the owner's decision, 2026-10-23): a branch is reviewed on its lease's preview URL, never on a stage, and zcp steers a deploy from a lease to a stage towards main rather than refusing it. From a lease, the part's stage is any app-runtime target — anything but a pool, a Box or a managed service — whatever mode adopt recorded it in: a pool never pairs with a stage (PS-2) and adopt records a lone runtime beside it as `dev`, so the target's mode cannot tell. Such a deploy first fetches the remote's `main` into the lease's `origin/main` (`git fetch origin +refs/heads/main:refs/remotes/origin/main`, inside the source's git lock, bounded, through the credential helper the lease's repository keeps — DP-6), so `sha="origin/main"` ships main as the remote has it now; a fetch that fails is no failure. After resolving what it ships it asks `git merge-base --is-ancestor <commit> origin/main`. It is never refused for that commit: a commit not on `origin/main`, or uncommitted changes, deploys like any other — a corrective deploy is never gated, a failed stage must take a fix before that fix is merged, and merging is not the agent's call. Its result then carries one `warnings` entry: stage tracks main, what the deploy ships (never what the stage runs — the build may fail, GF-5) and that it is not on `origin/main`, with main's short sha — or, for uncommitted changes on top of a commit that is on `origin/main`, that the commit is on it and only the uncommitted changes are not; a failed fetch, so the compare used the copy the lease fetched last; the lease's preview URL as the place to review a branch, through `zerops_dev_server action=status hostname=<alias>` (a lease deploy runs no supervisor command in the leased container, so the URL is not read here); and the call that puts main back, `zerops_deploy sourceService=<alias> targetService=<stage> sha="origin/main"`. Where zcp cannot tell — no `origin/main`, no commit, a check that gave no answer — the entry says so and why. A commit on `origin/main` (clean) adds nothing. Every other deploy — a lease to a pool (DP-7), any deploy from a service — fetches nothing and says nothing more. The git-push refusal from a lease (DP-6) points at the preview URL and at putting main on the stage, never at sending the branch there. `TestDeploySSH_StageTracksMain`, `TestDeploySSH_StageTracksMain_ChecksTheShippedCommit`, `TestDeployTool_LeaseToStage_StageTracksMain`, `TestDeployTool_LeaseGitPushRefusal_SteersStageToMain`, `TestLease_Integration_DeployToStage_SaysStageTracksMain`. |
| DP-9 | A full pool refuses an acquire at once (`all-reserved`, retryable) and queues nothing — an acquire succeeds only after a release (live-verified 2026-10-23). The orchestrator passes the platform's cause on (`meta[].metadata.message`), kept as `SwarmError.Cause`. The refusal reads the pool's limits and containers (both answer at once) and says how many of at most how many are leased (a container `reserved`, or `stopping`/`starting` under its holder's park or unpark, PS-7), which leases are zcp's own (aliases; each reserved container zcp has a record of is renewed with its lease, DP-2: one another holder now reserves is theirs, never named or read, and one the orchestrator does not answer about is named zcp's, `unverified`, never read) and how many other holders hold, and what frees a container: release a lease, or raise `maxContainers` while it is below 10 (`zerops_scale` refuses a higher value, PS-4), an offer that says a raise is an infrastructure change to ask the person about first and that each container it adds needs its preview port declared in the pool's `run.ports` first (PS-17, `TestLeaseAcquire_FullPool_RaiseOfferIsThePersonsCallAndNeedsPreviewPorts`) — at 10, that raising will not help. A cause other than the service maximum is quoted, and raising is not offered. `all-reserved` never reads "may succeed in a moment". With ssh and the pool's listing the refusal reads each of zcp's leases the renew confirms as status does (DP-21: all at once, each within its budget, changing nothing; with no listing zcp's records are named and never read, since a container whose lease ended may be another holder's) and lists them oldest activity first — those last active longest ago (marked `idle` past 24 h, DP-25), then the more recent, then those in use now (a preview connection open), then those whose activity was not read — each with what a release would lose (its uncommitted or untracked files and commits on no remote branch, counted). The release it names is the oldest of them that holds nothing unsaved and is not in use (the first otherwise), after asking the person when this conversation did not take it; it never names `force`. A listed container no lease holds that no acquire takes still takes a place of the pool — one the orchestrator is removing (`lockState` `deleting`), or one in any state but `ACTIVE` or `STOPPED` (a platform action on it failed, `ACTION_FAILED`) — and the refusal names each by alias with its state: one being removed frees its place within about half a minute (acquire again then); a failed one zcp cannot remove — the orchestrator removes only a container its holder leases, its `force` takes the pool's `ADMIN_TOKEN` (PS-6), the platform's API has no container delete, and the holder's own delete left one standing (live, 2026-10-25: webpool #5 went `ACTION_FAILED` after a restart its exhausted process table could not run, zcp's release removed it with its lease, and five minutes later it was still listed and every acquire refused "full: 2 of at most 3 containers are leased") — so the refusal names `zerops_events` for what failed and, below the ceiling, raising `maxContainers` by one as the person's call; zcp never acts on it (`TestLeaseAcquire_FullPool_NamesTheFailedContainerItCounts`). `TestSwarmClient_ErrorEnvelope_KeepsThePlatformCause`, `TestLeaseAcquire_FullPool_SaysHowFullAndWhatFreesOne`, `TestLeaseAcquire_FullPool_ListsYourLeasesOldestActivityFirst`, `TestLeaseTool_Acquire_FullPool_SaysHowFull`, `TestLeaseTool_Acquire_FullPool_ListsYourLeasesOldestActivityFirst`, `TestLeaseOwnership_FullPool_NamesNoLeaseAnotherHolderTook`, `TestLease_Integration_FullAndStoppedPools`, `TestLease_Integration_IdleStatusAndFullPoolByActivity`, `TestLeaseAcquire_FullPool_ContainerBeingParkedIsLeased`. |
| DP-10 | A stopped pool's orchestrator does not answer (the address resolves, nothing listens); a restart or a stop and start keeps every container's id, number, hostname and lease, its `/var/www` and `/home/zerops`, and loses processes and `/tmp` (live-verified 2026-10-23). When the orchestrator does not answer, zcp reads the pool's status by id (the search lags): a pool that is not running is reported as such — stopped, with `zerops_manage action=start` and what a stop keeps and loses; starting, with "retry once ACTIVE" — never as zcp running outside the project, which stays the answer for a running pool (DP-5). `TestLeaseOps_PoolNotRunning_SaysStartIt`, `TestLeaseTool_StoppedPool_SaysStartIt`. |
| DP-11 | `action=status` without a pool never fails over one pool that cannot be read (stopped, unreachable, no bearer): that pool's leases are zcp's records, state `held` and `unverified` with why (`unverified` also marks a lease whose renew got no answer while its pool's listing did, DP-2: its state is then the listing's — `held`, `parked`, `unmounted` — never `lost`, and nothing is read from it), the pool is named under `unreadPools`, every other pool is reconciled as usual, and the envelope's `leases[]` marks only that pool's leases `unverified` (`docs/spec-mate.md` §2.8a.3). A named pool that cannot be read stays an error. Status — and the envelope's lease reader, which runs after every tool result in a project with a pool — lists the project's services once (an ES-backed search, §3.5) and resolves every pool and the warm counts (DP-27) from that one list, never a search per pool (`TestLeaseStatus_ListsTheServicesOnce`). `TestLeaseList_NoPool_OneStoppedPool_OthersStillVerified`, `TestLeaseTool_Status_OneStoppedPool_OthersStillListed`, `TestLeaseTool_Ownership_RenewFails_UnverifiedNeverRead`, `TestLease_Integration_FullAndStoppedPools`. |
| DP-12 | A lease of a repository on this Mate's HQ — `ops.IsHQRemote` against the address of the HQ its enrollment names, read at each acquire — authenticates as HQ's git user `mate`: the checkout's fetch and the helper the repository keeps answer `username=mate` with the session's `ZCP_HQ_GIT_TOKEN` — the pool's HQ credential (DP-16), never its `GIT_TOKEN` — and nothing without it. The helper is stored under HQ's scope, its port included when it is not 443, by the rule a dev service's checkout follows. Unlike a dev service's HQ helper it never asks `zcp hq git-credential`: zcp is not installed in a pool container. Any other repository keeps DP-6's helper. The lease record keeps the canonical repository, never a credential, so for a lease on HQ the release guard (DP-4) and the git-push refusal from a lease (DP-6) name HQ's branch rule: a branch of the agent's own (`git checkout -b <branch>` first when on `main`), never `main`, never forced. Inside a Mate an acquire puts the Mate credential on the pool's `GIT_TOKEN` first (DP-16). A lease of an HQ repository whose session still finds the pool's `ZCP_HQ_GIT_TOKEN` unset or another token — zcp outside a Mate, or a secret changed or revoked since zcp wrote it — fails at checkout, hands the container back, and says what is missing and that an acquire inside a Mate writes it afresh, as `PREREQUISITE_MISSING` — never as `GIT_TOKEN_*`, whose contract asks the user for a token of their own. `TestLeaseCheckoutCommand_HQRepo_AuthenticatesAsMate`, `TestLeaseHQCredentialHelper_AnswersAsMateFromSessionEnvOnly`, `TestLeaseAcquire_HQRepo_ChecksOutAsMate`, `TestLeaseCheckout_HQCredentialFailure_NamesTheMateCredential`, `TestLeaseRelease_HQRepo_HintNamesHQsBranchRule`, `TestLeaseTool_HQRepo_ChecksOutAsMate`, `TestLeaseTool_HQCheckoutFailure_SaysWhatIsMissing`, `TestDeployTool_LeaseGitPushRefusal_HQRepo_NamesHQsBranchRule`, `TestLease_Integration_HQRepo_ChecksOutAsMateAndPushesByHQsRule`. |
| DP-13 | A lease always has its supervisor. A pool container released with `reset=true` comes back as its image — `$HOME`, `/var/www`, writes under `/opt`, `/tmp` and every process gone — and the platform never runs its `run.start` again, not after a stop and start of the container nor a restart of the pool (live-verified 2026-10-23); since every release resets (DP-4), a reused container would run no `zcp-devsup`. Live: 22 of 22 reused leases ran none, and starting it over ssh brought their dev servers back on the right preview port. So zcp starts it: an acquire, after its checkout and the repository's prepare (DP-14), an unpark (DP-28), and a `zerops_dev_server` call on a lease whose read finds `zcp-devsup` installed but nothing answering its control socket (a reused container restarted since, a supervisor that died) — once per call, then it reads again. After a checkout a supervisor that already answers — `run.start`'s, in a container the platform just created, which booted on an empty `/var/www` — is reloaded, by a call of its own after the servers' overlay (DP-22), so the declared servers start as in a reused container; a refused reload is a note. The start itself carries neither the overlay nor the reload. A dev-server call never reloads (a checkout missing the declaration would stop every server). The start is `run.start` by hand: `zcp-devsup run` in `/var/www` as the ssh session's user, with the session's env (the pool's service env and `ZEROPS_StableNumber`, DP-6), in its own session (`setsid`; `nohup` where there is none) with stdin from `/dev/null` and its output appended to `$HOME/.zcp/devsup/supervisor.log`, so it outlives the ssh call; it waits up to 10 s for the control socket to answer. It starts one only when `zcp-devsup ctl status` exits `2` (no supervisor) — one that answers, or holds the socket without answering, never gets a second beside it — and, where `flock` exists, under a lock in `$HOME/.zcp/devsup`, so concurrent calls start one. A pool whose image carries no `zcp-devsup` (a pool used as a sandbox for running code) gets nothing started: the acquire's note says the pool is not a dev pool — it serves no dev servers or previews — and `zerops_dev_server` refuses its leases. A supervisor that does not come up never fails the acquire: the lease stays held and its note carries the supervisor's output; a dev-server call answers with it. `zerops_verify` and `zerops_dev_server` on a lease reach it only once its orchestrator vouches for it (DP-2), so a supervisor is never started in another holder's container. `zerops_verify` on a lease only reads: a supervisor that does not answer is named as one `zerops_dev_server` starts, never blamed on the pool's `run.start`. A dev service is never started this way: its supervisor is its dev setup's `run.start` and a missing one moves the dev setup onto it (docs/spec-mate.md §2.8a.2). `TestEnsureLeaseSupervisor_StartsOneOnlyWhenNoneAnswers`, `TestEnsureLeaseSupervisor_StartsItAsRunStartWouldAndDetached`, `TestEnsureLeaseSupervisor_ConcurrentCallsStartOne`, `TestEnsureLeaseSupervisor_CommandQuotesItsWorkDir`, `TestEnsureLeaseSupervisor_LeavesARunningOneAlone`, `TestLeaseAcquire_ReloadsASupervisorThatAlreadyRuns`, `TestLeaseAcquire_EnsuresTheSupervisorAfterTheCheckout`, `TestDevServer_SupervisorStartedByZcpOnlyInALease`, `TestDevServer_LeaseSupervisorMissingOrNotComingUp_SaysWhy`, `TestLeaseDevServerChecks_NoSupervisor_SaysWhatALeaseNeeds`, `TestLeaseTool_Acquire_EnsuresTheSupervisor`, `TestDevServerTool_LeaseWhoseSupervisorIsGone_StartsIt`, `TestLease_Integration_ReusedContainerGetsItsSupervisor`, `TestLease_Integration_PoolWithoutZcpDevsup_SaysNotADevPool`. |
| DP-14 | A pool image bakes the part's dependencies into `/var/www` through `run.prepareCommands` (they survive a reset, and the checkout's `git clean -fd` keeps them: they are ignored); a branch whose lockfile differs needs an incremental install on top (live: `npm install --prefer-offline` took 1.0–1.6 s and left the tree clean). The repository declares it as `prepare`, an optional top-level string in `.zcp/dev-servers.json` (docs/spec-mate.md §2.8a.1). After a successful checkout an acquire reads the checked-out declaration and, when it declares one, runs it in `/var/www` as an ssh call of its own — `/bin/sh -c`, the session's env, stdin from `/dev/null`, bounded at 5 min — before the supervisor step (DP-13), which starts the declared servers. Its failure never hands the container back and is never classified as a checkout failure (`classifyCheckoutFailure`, DP-6): the lease stays held, and the result's `prepare` carries the command, `ok`, the exit code and its output's tail, with a note saying how to run it again. A declaration that cannot be read runs nothing and the note says why. zcp keeps `prepare` when it rewrites the declaration and never writes one; the supervisor ignores it. A workspace monorepo's install reads every member's manifest, the workspace definition, the lockfile and any patch directory, and `run.prepareCommands` see only what `build.addToRunPrepare` copies (zerops-docs build pipeline: the prepare runtime container holds no application code), so the pool's setup lists them all there and installs the whole workspace: a package manager that checks the installed tree before a script reads a filtered install as stale — pnpm 11 with a closure-keeping `--filter`, live 2026-10-24 — and its next `run` or `exec` in a lease starts a full install; a part's checks can import sources beyond its own package closure, whose dependencies the image then needs too (pools-and-box theme, `TestThemes_PoolsAndBox_DevPool_WorkspaceDependencies_FactsPresent`). `TestDeclaration_Prepare_ParsesAndRoundTrips`, `TestDeclareDevServer_KeepsTheRepositorysPrepare`, `TestLeaseAcquire_RunsTheRepositorysPrepareAfterTheCheckout`, `TestLeasePrepare_FailureIsNeverACheckoutFailure`, `TestLeaseTool_Acquire_ReportsTheRepositorysPrepare`, `TestLease_Integration_ReusedContainerGetsItsSupervisor`. |
| DP-15 | A container the platform created for the pool's `minContainers` has no primary snapshot: `release?reset=true` answers 400 `containerAction`, reason `no-primary-snapshot`, not retryable, and the reservation stays (live-verified 2026-10-23). zcp never releases it unreset — a clean acquire resets only a container marked dirty, which writes over ssh never mark, so the next holder would get this one's `/var/www`, `$HOME`, `/tmp` and `/opt`. Once the release guard passed (DP-4) or with `force`, it deletes the container through the orchestrator (`DELETE /container/{id}`, the lease only in the `X-Swarm-Lease` header, DP-3; 202, gone in about 7 s), drops the record and says so: the container could not be reset, so it was deleted; set the pool's `minContainers` to 0, since zcp keeps the pool's warm container itself (DP-27), one the orchestrator creates and can reset — never that the agent pre-warm containers by hand. The pool creates fresh containers, with a snapshot, on later acquires. A refused delete keeps the reservation and the record and names both refusals; a lease the orchestrator no longer knows is dropped as lost, and so is one whose delete is refused `held-by-other` once a renew agrees the lease ended (DP-2). A failed checkout's hand-back (DP-6) takes the same path, and its refusal says where the container went in its message and its suggestion alike — released with a reset, deleted because it could not be reset, gone already, or still reserved with the release to run first — never "released" beside "stays reserved". `TestLeaseRelease_Unresettable_DeletesTheContainer`, `TestLeaseRelease_UnresettableAndTheDeleteFails_KeepsTheLease`, `TestLeaseCheckout_FailureOnAnUnresettableContainer_DeletesIt`, `TestAbandonLease_MessageAndSuggestionSayWhereTheContainerWent`, `TestLeaseTool_Release_Unresettable_DeletesWithTheLease`, `TestLease_Integration_UnresettableContainerIsDeletedNotReleasedUnreset`. |
| DP-16 | Inside a Mate (`runtime.Info.MateEnabled`) an acquire of a repository on its HQ (DP-12) makes the pool's `ZCP_HQ_GIT_TOKEN` (`ops.PoolHQCredentialEnvKey`) the Mate credential its HQ enrollment holds now, before it takes a container — as git-push-setup gives it to a wired pair's dev service (`spec-mate.md` §10.10, *The credential*): the hand-rolled userData write, `sensitive:true`. It is a variable of its own, never the pool's `GIT_TOKEN`: every container of a pool reads the pool's whole service env, and a lease of a repository outside HQ answers its host's 401 with `GIT_TOKEN` — so with the Mate credential there it reached github.com at the checkout's fetch, in a warm clone and on every push, and an HQ acquire wrote over a `GIT_TOKEN` the user had set, under the leases still holding it. Only the HQ lease helper reads `ZCP_HQ_GIT_TOKEN` — inline for an HQ repository's fetch and warm clone, persisted under HQ's scope alone — so the credential reaches no other host, the user's `GIT_TOKEN` is never written, and both kinds of repository work on one pool at once (`TestLeaseAcquire_HQThenOutsideHQ_TheMateCredentialReachesOnlyHQ`, `TestLeaseAcquire_HQRepo_TheUsersGitTokenStays`). Like `GIT_TOKEN` it is masked at every echo site, kept out of a generated `.env`, of export and launch bundles and of Mate's vault (`TestPoolHQCredentialVariable_IsACredentialEverywhere`). The Mate's API key writes a sensitive value but reads every one back `REDACTED` (live-verified 2026-10-23), so zcp compares by its own record, never by reading: `.zcp/state/leases/credentials/<pool>.json` (mode 0600) keeps the pool service id and the `hq.CredentialFingerprint` of what zcp wrote — never the credential. It writes when that record is absent, names another pool service (re-created under the hostname) or another fingerprint (the Mate enrolled again), and skips the write when it matches — or, for a pool that keys a pair, when the record the repository pass took over onto the pair (below) names the pool service and the credential: a write deletes the variable and creates it anew, and between the two every fresh session of every container of the pool reads none, so git in the pool's other leases fails "could not read Username" (live, 2026-10-25: a sync's push from one lease failed 2 s after another conversation's acquire rewrote the credential its pool, keying a pair, already held, while a push just before and a sync 12 s after went through; `TestLeaseTool_HQRepo_PoolKeyingAPair_NoWriteOfTheCredentialItHolds`). A pool's service env reaches its containers' fresh ssh sessions within seconds, no restart (live-verified 2026-10-23), so an acquire that wrote reads `sha256sum` of `$ZCP_HQ_GIT_TOKEN` in fresh sessions of the leased container — the hash, never the value — until it is the credential's, at most 8 reads 3 s apart (a session that cannot say ends the wait), and then checks out. An enrollment holding no credential zcp can read refuses before any container is taken (`PREREQUISITE_MISSING`), and so does a write that fails (with the write's own code). A checkout HQ refuses drops the record, so the next acquire writes afresh. The repository pass — bootstrap, adopt, every develop start — renews every pool whose record names another fingerprint than the enrollment's, in a Mate with pairs or without: a lease is held until released, so the next acquire may be far off while the pool's leases need the new credential from their next fresh session. It reads the pool by id, drops the record of a pool that is gone (a 404 or a 400 `serviceStackNotFound`, `TestRenewPoolHQCredentials_PoolGoneBy400_RecordDropped`), and reports one it could not write, retried on the next pass. The credential never appears in a result, error, log, envelope, session command or state file. A repository outside HQ, or zcp outside a Mate, writes no credential on the pool, and nothing zcp does ever writes the pool's `GIT_TOKEN`: it stays the user's (DP-6). A pool that keys a pair (§13 PM-10) is renewed with its pair, from the pair's HQ credential fingerprint, into the same `ZCP_HQ_GIT_TOKEN` — a dev service keeps its Mate credential as `GIT_TOKEN`, a pool never (`TestHQPass_PoolDevHalf_RenewalLeavesThePoolsGitTokenTheUsers`): the pass takes the per-pool record over when it names the pool service the pair records, and removes it. `TestLeaseAcquire_HQRepoInAMate_PutsTheMateCredentialOnThePool`, `TestLeaseAcquire_PoolTokenStaysTheUsers`, `TestLeaseAcquire_HQRepo_WaitsUntilASessionSeesTheWrittenCredential`, `TestLeaseAcquire_HQRepo_NoCredentialOnThePool_TakesNoContainer`, `TestLeaseAcquire_HQRepo_RefusedCheckout_WritesAfreshNextTime`, `TestRenewPoolHQCredentials_RewritesEveryPoolWhoseFingerprintDiffers`, `TestCredentialFingerprint_IsTheClientsFingerprint`, `TestLeaseTool_HQRepoInAMate_PutsTheMateCredentialOnThePool`, `TestReconcileHQRepositories_RenewsThePoolsCredentialAfterTheMateEnrollsAgain`, `TestLease_Integration_HQRepoInAMate_PutsTheMateCredentialOnThePool`, `TestHQPass_PoolDevHalf_TakesOverThePoolsCredentialRecord`. |
| DP-17 | A branch that spans several app parts holds one lease per part's pool: a **group**, derived, never stored — the leases zcp holds (by its records) of one repository and branch; a record older than keeping `repo` is of its pool's default repository, an empty branch is `main`. zcp holds **one lease per repository, branch and pool** (`main` included): an acquire of a key it already holds returns that lease as status reports it (`held`, or `unmounted` with what it lacks), `reused: true`, and reserves, checks out and starts nothing — so a second conversation's acquire of a held branch shares its container, and `reused` — a field, since a client acts on fields — tells it to bind nothing. A record of the key whose lease ended (`lost`), with none held beside it, refuses the acquire naming the release that drops it (DP-2: a release drops a lost record without asking the orchestrator, DP-4) — one whose container another holder now reserves is such a record (live 4g, where status had called it `held` and the acquire kept refusing a branch no release could free). A record of the key the orchestrator does not answer about (`unverified`, DP-2) refuses the acquire too, naming `zerops_lease action=status pool=<pool>`, which asks again — never returned `reused`, since its container may be another holder's. The held-lease check, the reservation, the record and the checkout run under a lock per key (`<stateDir>/locks/lease-<hash>.lock`, flock, waited for until the call ends), so two acquires of one key reserve one container and the one that waited returns it reused, while acquires of other keys run at once; a state directory that cannot be written refuses before any container is taken. `action=acquire pools=[…]` (`pool` is one pool of it) first checks every pool (one service listing: a pool named twice, an unknown service, a service that is no pool, a pool with no bearer — DP-3), the branch and one repository — `repo`, else the default every pool that has one agrees on (pools defaulting to different repositories are refused naming each, with "pass repo"; a pool with no default takes the group's) — and, for every member it will lease, the HQ credential (DP-16), all before reserving any container; then reserves the missing members at once (each DP-3's own deadline: an acquire takes 14–29 s, so one after another would add them up); then checks out the first new member alone, cut — when the branch is new to origin — from the group's recorded base, else from what its own fetch found on `origin/main`, and the other new members at once from the commit the first was cut from (DP-6), so every member starts from one main commit; then each runs the repository's prepare and the supervisor step (DP-13, DP-14) once the keys are let go. A member waits for every reservation and the first checkout — minutes on a pool still preparing a container — so each is asked about (DP-2) right before its checkout, and again before its record is written after it: one whose lease ended meanwhile, its container maybe taken again under the same alias, is never checked out or written over, its record dropped while it is still its own and its container never handed back; one the orchestrator does not answer about is not checked out; either fails the acquire as any failing member does. Every member goes through the one lease's steps — never a second copy of them. If any member fails (refused reservation, record write, checkout), every container this call reserved goes back as a failed checkout's does (DP-15: unmount, release with reset, or delete when it cannot be reset) while the leases held before the call stay, and the refusal is the first failing member's own (in the pools' order), carrying every other member's failure, where each other container went, and which held leases stay; one that could not be handed back stays reserved and recorded, and the suggestion names `zerops_lease action=release lease=<alias> force=true`. The result lists the members in the pools' order — the order Mate binds the first of — and never a lease string (DP-3). Every view of a lease built from zcp's record — acquire, status, release, park — and its envelope snapshot name the group's repository as `repo` beside `branch`: the record's identity, canonical and credential-free (`topology.RepoIdentity`: no userinfo, query or fragment), absent for a lease zcp holds no record of or an older record naming none — so a client keys a group as zcp does, never by branch alone (docs/spec-mate.md §6.3; `TestLeaseViews_CarryTheRepository`, `TestComputeEnvelope_LeaseRecords_CarryTheRepository`). `ops.LeaseGroup(alias)` answers a lease's group from the records, one per pool. `TestLeaseAcquireGroup_AllPoolsCheckedBeforeAnyReservation`, `TestLeaseAcquireGroup_PoolsWithDifferentDefaultRepos_Refused`, `TestLeaseAcquireGroup_OneDefaultRepo_IsTheGroups`, `TestLeaseAcquireGroup_MembersInPoolsOrder`, `TestLeaseAcquireGroup_ReservesInParallel`, `TestLeaseAcquireGroup_OneMemberFails_HandsBackThisCallsMembers`, `TestLeaseAcquireGroup_NewBranch_EveryMemberCutFromOneBase`, `TestLeaseAcquireGroup_BranchOnOrigin_RecordsNoBase`, `TestLeaseAcquire_HeldBranchOnPool_ReturnsHeldLeaseReused`, `TestLeaseAcquire_LostLeaseOfTheBranch_RefusedNamingTheRelease`, `TestLeaseOwnership_Acquire_ReuseCheck`, `TestLeaseAcquire_ConcurrentSameKey_ReservesOnce`, `TestLeaseAcquire_ConcurrentDifferentKeys_RunTogether`, `TestLeaseAcquire_StateDirUnwritable_TakesNoContainer`, `TestLeaseGroup_HeldLeasesOfOneRepositoryAndBranch`, `TestLeaseTool_GroupAcquire_MembersInPoolsOrder`, `TestLeaseTool_GroupAcquire_BadInput_TypedRefusal`, `TestLease_Integration_GroupAcquire_OneLeasePerPoolInPoolsOrder`, `TestLeaseAcquireGroup_MemberEndsWhileAnotherWaits_NeverCheckedOutOrWrittenOver`, `TestLeaseAcquire_RecordTakenDuringTheCheckout_NeverWrittenOver`. |
| DP-18 | A lease clones its repository at the URL exactly as it was given — trimmed of surrounding space, still https without userinfo (DP-6) — and so does an acquire that takes the pool's default, which zcp records as given: a plain git server answers `…/repo.git` and refuses `…/repo` ("repository not found", live Mate run 2026-10-23), so a clone never goes through `topology.CanonicalRepoURL`. | DP-19 | A dev-server call on a lease — every action — first asks the pool's orchestrator whether the container is still the lease's (DP-2). | DP-20 | A pool service that is gone or being deleted holds nothing of a lease, and its orchestrator does not answer. The release's own pool read and status read a search miss the same way: found by id, the release goes to the orchestrator and status reconciles the pool; a read by id that fails otherwise is an unread pool (DP-11) — a lease is never `lost`, nor its record dropped, on the search's word alone. | DP-21 | `action=status` reports, for each lease zcp holds that it reads — `held` or `unmounted`, confirmed as its own by the renew of its lease (DP-2), its ssh alias installed — `activity` and `unsaved`, from **one** ssh invocation per lease, every lease at once, each bounded by 5 s (`leaseActivityReadTimeout`). `activity.lastChangeAt` is the checkout's last change: the newest of `.git/logs/HEAD`'s mtime and the newest mtime of any modified or untracked file git does not ignore (`git ls-files -z -m -o --exclude-standard`); `activity.acquiredAt` is the record's, `activity.readAt` zcp's clock at the read; DP-25 draws a lease's idleness from these facts. `previewLastConnAt`/`previewOpenConns` come from the lease's supervisor in the same invocation, after the guard's reads and whatever they did: `ctl version` and `ctl status`, verbs every `zcp-devsup` knows, the status bounded at 2 s where the container has `timeout`; the read still exits with the checkout part's status. zcp takes the preview server's two fields (`docs/spec-mate.md` §2.8a.2) only from a supervisor whose `ctl version` lists `activity`; an older one, none, or one that does not answer leaves both absent, and `activity.note` says the preview was not observed and why. One that lists `activity` and runs no dev server on a preview port has no preview forward, so no preview connection can be open: it reads `previewOpenConns: 0` with no `previewLastConnAt` and no note, so a client drawing idleness from the envelope reads the preview as observed, as DP-25 does (`TestLeaseActivity_NoPreviewServer_ZeroOpen`). `previewOpenConns` 0 is an observed zero; `previewLastConnAt` is absent while no connection came since the supervisor started. `unsaved` is the release guard's own two reads (DP-4), built and parsed by the same code the guard runs, so status and release agree: the first 20 `git status --porcelain` lines as `files`, all of them counted in `fileCount`, and `unpushedCommits`; a clean checkout reads `{"fileCount":0,"unpushedCommits":0}`. With nothing modified the activity line's second field is empty (`"<epoch> "`). Live-verified 2026-10-23: 62/65 ms median/p95 over zcp's ssh options (5 ms inside the container), 118/156 ms on a 10,000-file checkout with 150 changes; a deleted tracked file and a rename (`mv` keeps the mtime) do not move `lastChangeAt`, `git stash -u` does (it writes `logs/HEAD`). A fact that was not read is absent, never zero: an unreachable container, a read cut at its budget, a failed `git status` or count (in git's own words), a lease its orchestrator did not answer about (`unverified`, DP-2, DP-11) or one missing its alias leaves `lastChangeAt` and `unsaved` absent and says why in `activity.note`, keeping what an earlier part read; a read never fails status. A lease whose container did not answer the read is marked `notAnswering` with why — its process limit (its ssh server could not start the shell: fork answered EAGAIN, "resource temporarily unavailable", the call exiting 127) or ssh not reaching it (exit 255) — and `nextActions` names it and says to tell the person rather than wait on it or retry in a loop: a container at its process limit does not recover while what fills it runs, and a restart of its pool restarts every container of it, the other leases' dev servers too (live, 2026-10-25: a full production build filled webpool-5's process table while its dev server still served; a helper looped `until ssh webpool-5 …` for 20 minutes, its orchestrator waited on it, and the dev-server call said only `exit status 127`). Every call that runs in a lease says the same instead of a bare ssh exit: a dev-server call (DP-19), a sync's source and each member (DP-24), and the release guard (DP-4), which releases a lease at its process limit only with `force`, after the person agreed (`TestLeaseTool_ProcessLimit_SaysTheLeaseIsNotAnswering`, `TestDevServerTool_Lease_ProcessLimit_SaysNotAnswering`). Only a call with ssh reads it, so the envelope's lease reader never does. `TestLeaseActivity_CommandIsReadOnly`, `TestLeaseActivity_ParsesNewestChange`, `TestLeaseActivity_ReadFails_SaysWhatFailedAndKeepsWhatItRead`, `TestLeaseActivity_RealCheckout_ReadsWithoutWritingAndAgreesWithTheGuard`, `TestLeaseActivity_RealCheckout_NotACheckout_SaysGitStatusFailed`, `TestLeaseActivity_JSON_AbsentFactIsNeverZero`, `TestLeaseStatus_ReportsActivityAndUnsavedPerHeldLease`, `TestLeaseStatus_ActivityUnreadable_SaysWhy`, `TestLeaseStatus_ActivityReadsInParallelWithinBudget`, `TestLeaseStatus_ActivityNotRead_SaysWhyOrStaysAbsent`, `TestLeaseActivity_PreviewFromSupervisorWhenCapable`, `TestLeaseActivity_OlderSupervisor_PreviewNotObserved`, `TestLeaseActivity_RealShell_SupervisorAnswersAfterGitFails_KeepsGitsFailure`, `TestLeaseTool_Status_CarriesActivity`, `TestLease_Integration_StatusReportsActivityAndUnsaved`. |
| DP-22 | A part's dev servers reach the same branch's sibling workspaces by container hostname: over the project network `http://<container hostname>:<port>` answers from a container of another pool, on a port in that pool's `run.ports` and on one not in it alike (live-verified 2026-10-23). | DP-23 | `action=release branch=<branch>` (optional `repo` and `force`; never with `lease`) releases the branch's group (DP-17) — every lease zcp holds, by its records, of the branch of one repository — whole or not at all. `repo` empty is the one repository zcp leases the branch for; a branch it leases for several is refused naming each with its leases ("pass repo"), and a branch it holds no lease of is refused, both before any container is read. | DP-24 | `action=sync lease=<alias>` moves the commits the agent made in one lease to every other lease of its branch's group (DP-17), through origin — where review reads the branch and the release guard counts unpushed work (DP-4) — never lease to lease: the agent commits over `ssh <alias>` (zcp stages and commits nothing, GF-2) and the sync pushes and fast-forwards. It takes `lease` alone: `pool`, `branch`, `repo` and `force=true` are refused (the branch and its leases are the lease's own; a sync never forces). It refuses, changing nothing anywhere, a lease of `main` (an empty branch is `main`; main changes only through review), a zcp without ssh into its leases, a source whose container is no longer its lease's (named with the release that drops its record) or that the orchestrator does not answer about (DP-2), a source checkout holding uncommitted or untracked files — named as the release guard's own read names them (DP-4), with the commit to make — or whose HEAD is not on the lease's branch, and a push that fails: origin moved on (`GIT_PUSH_NON_FAST_FORWARD`: the agent integrates `origin/<branch>` there and syncs again), origin's rules refused the ref (`PERMISSION_DENIED`, nothing wrong with the credential; for a repository on HQ, HQ's branch rule, DP-12), a missing or refused credential (DP-6's classification, `GIT_TOKEN_*` outside HQ), or a source ssh cannot reach. The push is `git push --porcelain [-u] origin refs/heads/<branch>:refs/heads/<branch>` — `-u` while the branch does not track `origin/<branch>` — never a force flag or a `+` refspec, authenticating through the helper the lease's checkout persisted (DP-6); its outcome is git's own flag: `pushed`, `new-branch` or `up-to-date`. Then every other lease of the group (`ops.LeaseGroup`: one per pool, earliest acquired first), all at once, on its branch: `git fetch origin refs/heads/<branch>`, then — HEAD at origin's commit: `up-to-date`; HEAD with commits origin's lacks: `has-own-commits` with their count (`ownCommits`), never rebased or merged; otherwise `git merge --ff-only` to origin's commit: `fast-forwarded` (`from`, `to`), or, when git refuses because the merge would overwrite a local change, `local-changes-in-the-way`, its files named by the release guard's read. Each lease is asked about first (DP-2): one no longer its lease's is `lost`, naming the release that drops its record, and one the orchestrator does not answer about `unverified`, naming the sync again once it answers — neither fetched nor fast-forwarded, nothing sent to it over ssh. The group is read before the push, so a lease's alias must still name a lease of the sync's repository and branch when it is asked about: one released since and taken again under the alias for another repository or branch is `lost` as well, whatever the orchestrator answers the lease it names now. A checkout on another branch is `on-another-branch`, a container ssh cannot reach `unreachable`, any other failure `failed` with git's words and the checkout classification's `code`; each of these is left exactly as it was and carries a note saying what to do. Up to date or fast-forwarded, a branch that tracks nothing is set to track `origin/<branch>`. A lease's outcome never fails the sync: the push already happened. Every read-only git call runs with `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0` (DP-4); only the push, the fetch, the fast-forward and the upstream write. A fast-forwarded lease names in `unwatchedServers` only the running servers nothing zcp knows of reloads — its runtime entries (`ctl run`), which no declaration covers — each with its `zerops_dev_server action=restart`, since they keep serving the code they started with. A declared server reloads by itself, by the declaration's own contract (docs/spec-mate.md §2.8a.1 `servers[].watch`): with a `watch` the supervisor restarts it on a change under its paths, and without one its command's own watcher (`--watch`, vite) does, so a sync never names one nor suggests restarting it (measured 2026-10-24: a sync named a Vite server that had already served the fast-forwarded file, and obeying would have cost a 25–74 s restart of the heaviest server). A fast-forward that changes `.zcp/dev-servers.json` (the step answers it, from `git diff --quiet` of the two commits) is zcp's own change to the lease's checkout, as an acquire's checkout is (DP-13), so the lease's supervisor takes it in the same call: the sibling overlay first when it changed (DP-22: computed and sent under the group's lock, DP-26; only to a supervisor that lists `env`, the restarts it sets off awaited), then `ctl reload` — an added server starts, a removed one stops, a changed one restarts — and the lease carries `reloaded: true` with a note naming what started, restarted and stopped; a declaration that cannot be read is never reloaded, and a supervisor that does not take it is a note naming the call that starts it on the new one. The servers that started or restarted since the fast-forward run the new code and are not named in `unwatchedServers`; restarting the others stays the agent's call. The result is `sync: {branch, source, commit, push: {outcome, setUpstream, ms}, members: [{alias, pool, outcome, from, to, ownCommits, unwatchedServers, reloaded, code, note, ms}], checkMs, ms}` — every step's duration in milliseconds — with `nextActions` naming each restart, each lease left as it was and, for a missing or refused credential, the user-owned credential contract; | DP-25 | Idleness is a label, never an act (`docs/spec-mate.md` §0 rule 4): nothing in zcp releases, stops or deletes a lease because it is idle. `action=status` marks a lease it read (DP-21) `idle` — `{since, next}`, on the result only, never on the envelope's `leases[]` — when nothing has happened in it for 24 h (`leaseIdleAfter`, the owner's answer): its last activity, the newest of `activity.lastChangeAt`, `previewLastConnAt` and `acquiredAt` (an acquire is activity), is 24 h or more before zcp's clock; no preview connection is open — `previewOpenConns` above 0 is in use now, however long ago that connection opened (a dev server's websocket stays open while its tab does); and every fact was read: the checkout's last change, the unsaved work, and the preview, from a supervisor that lists `activity` (one that runs no dev server on a preview port reads 0 open connections, DP-21). A fact not read — an older supervisor, one that does not answer or does not run, an unreachable container, a lease its orchestrator did not confirm — leaves the lease unmarked, never idle. The preview forward records no close, so once a long connection closes, idleness dates from when it opened: a person using a preview opens new connections (page loads, requests past keep-alive, the dev server's reconnect after any drop), so a connection open a day with no other beside it is a tab left open; the label can come early that way, never late while a connection is open, and every release still runs its guard. `since` is that last activity. `next` names, when the guard's reads find nothing a release would lose, `zerops_lease action=release lease=<alias>` and that files git ignores and stashes go with any release; with unsaved work, what it holds (the guard's own description: the first 20 files, all counted, and the commits on no remote branch) and how to keep it (commit and push it, HQ's branch rule on an HQ repository, or park it: `zerops_lease action=park lease=<alias>`, DP-28) — never a release and never `force`. The leases of a group (DP-17) are idle together or not at all: only when the call read every lease of the group (a status of one pool reads none of its leases on other pools) and each held one is idle — all marked since the newest activity of any, `next` naming the branch's release (DP-23, with `repo` when zcp leases the branch for several repositories) or what each lease holds. `nextActions` names the idle leases, that nothing releases a lease by itself, and to ask the person before releasing one this conversation did not take. Marking changes nothing: status calls no orchestrator mutation (a renew changes nothing, DP-2), writes no record or alias, runs no host command, and runs only the read-only checkout read (DP-21). `TestLeaseStatus_IdleNothingUnsaved_NamesTheRelease`, `TestLeaseStatus_ActiveOrUnread_NeverIdle`, `TestLeaseStatus_IdleWithUnsaved_NamesWhatAndNeverForce`, `TestLeaseStatus_GroupIdle_NamesTheBranchRelease`, `TestLeaseStatus_NeverMutates`, `TestLeaseTool_Status_IdleLease_NamesWhatFreesIt`, `TestLease_Integration_IdleStatusAndFullPoolByActivity`. |
| DP-26 | A released container goes to its pool's next acquirer under the same hostname (DP-1), so a sibling still wired to it would reach someone else's workspace. Every change zcp makes to a group's membership (DP-17) therefore first hands every other lease of the group the sibling overlay (DP-22) recomputed from zcp's records. An acquire that takes a lease of a branch zcp already leases — single or group — hands every lease of the group it did not take (held before it, returned `reused`, or not named) the overlay with the new ones in, after the checkout and beside the new leases' own supervisor step, which sends theirs; an acquire that takes none rewires nothing. `release lease=<alias>`, once its guard passed (DP-4) and before the orchestrator is asked, hands every other lease of the alias's group the overlay without it — unless the alias names another lease's record by then (DP-2), which may be a lease of the same branch; a release that then fails while the renew still answers the container the lease's own (DP-2) hands every lease the rewire changed the overlay back with the lease in, and the refusal says so — a container the orchestrator let go (the release went, the record could not be dropped), or one it does not answer about, is never handed back, and the refusal names the call that wires the siblings again. `release branch=<branch>` takes every lease of the group, so nothing is rewired before it; a lease the orchestrator would not release is handed the overlay without the leases that went. Every change of a group's membership holds the group's lock (`<stateDir>/locks/group-<hash>.lock` of the canonical repository and the branch, a flock): a release from its rewire through the drop of its record, a lost lease's release and a branch release likewise through their drops and the rewire of what stays held, and an acquire's rewire from reading zcp's records through its sends — so a rewire computed while a lease is still recorded is never sent after its release dropped it, and two releases of one group never leave a sibling wired to either. Every other overlay zcp sends a lease — a dev-server start's, an acquire's own supervisor step's, an unpark's, a sync's take of a changed declaration — is computed from the records and sent under the same lock, which is held for that read and the one `ctl env` exchange (bounded by `zcp-devsup ctl`'s 75 s), never for a supervisor's start or the wait for the restarts a changed overlay sets off: a group acquire's members still run their supervisor steps at once, and only their overlays' sends queue behind a release or a rewire of the group. A rewire runs per lease, all at once: read its dev servers, compute its overlay from its own declaration — one that cannot be read keeps only the variables still naming a container of the group, or the preview URL of a pool the group holds (DP-31) — and send it when it differs from the supervisor's, only to a supervisor whose `ctl version` lists `env` (one that does not is a note naming what it cannot set and the way to a newer one), then read `ctl status` until each server live before has been restarted under it, within the start budget. A lease whose image carries no zcp-devsup is left out. A parked lease (DP-28) is sent nothing — nothing answers on it — and is a note naming its unpark, which sends it the overlay its group then has. Each lease is asked about first (DP-2): one no longer its lease's is sent nothing and is a note naming the release that drops its record — its container may be another holder's — and one the orchestrator does not answer about is sent nothing, a note naming the call that sends the overlay once it answers. A lease ssh cannot reach, whose supervisor does not answer or refuses the overlay is a note naming it and the call that sends the overlay again (`zerops_dev_server action=restart hostname=<alias> name=<server>`), never a refusal: another lease's health never strands a container. The result carries, beside `leases`, `rewired: [{alias, siblingEnv, restarted, pending, note, ms}]` — `restarted` read back by those status reads — and `nextActions` repeats each note; no result carries a lease string (DP-3). A lease zcp drops as `lost` where no release rewires its branch — a park or an unpark whose stop or start found it ended, a re-park, a refused branch release — names the branch's leases that may still run wired to its container and the status that names the restart; nothing is sent to them. A lease that ends outside zcp is not chased (`docs/spec-mate.md` §0 rule 4): `zerops_lease action=status` marks a held lease `wiring: "stale"` when a server runs under an overlay — read from the `ctl status` its checkout read already runs (DP-21), from a supervisor that lists `env` — naming a container that is no lease of its group by that status's word (a group record its orchestrator gives as lost, or no record of the group) — or the preview URL of a pool on which the group holds no lease by that word (DP-31) — its note naming each such variable, the container, whose it was and the call that sends the overlay again; a status of one pool takes the other pools' records as they stand. `zerops_dev_server action=status` on a lease says the same (`wiring: "stale"`, its message) by zcp's records alone.
