## 11. Planned Features

### 11.1 Mode Expansion (simple/dev → standard)

**Status**: Partially implemented (ServiceMeta merge + awareness atom); generate/deploy flow for the new stage service is delegated to the agent via the `develop-mode-expansion` atom's guidance.

**Problem**: A service bootstrapped in simple or dev mode needs to expand to standard (dev+stage). This requires creating a new stage service, updating zerops.yaml with a stage entry, deploying to stage, and updating ServiceMeta.

**Mechanism**: Bootstrap in expansion mode — the existing runtime is flagged `isExisting: true` with `bootstrapMode: "standard"` and an explicit `stageHostname`. Plan example:

```json
{
  "runtime": {
    "devHostname": "app",
    "type": "bun@1.3",
    "isExisting": true,
    "bootstrapMode": "standard",
    "stageHostname": "appstage"
  }
}
```

What the engine guarantees:

1. **Meta merge** (`mergeExistingMeta`, via `writeProvisionMetas` / `writeBootstrapOutputs`): when an existing complete `ServiceMeta` is detected for the runtime hostname AND the target carries `IsExisting=true`, the about-to-be-written meta is merged with the existing one. Upgrade fields (`Mode`, the stage hostnames) come from the plan; user-authored fields (`BootstrappedAt`, `CloseDeployMode`, `CloseDeployModeConfirmed`, `GitPushState`, `RemoteURL`, `BuildIntegration`, `FirstDeployedAt`) are preserved, as are `PrimarySetupName` and each stage's setup (migrate-forward: a non-empty existing value wins) and `ProvisionedFromGit` (sticky-once-set OR). The authoritative preserved set lives in `mergeExistingMeta`. Without this, a dev→standard upgrade would silently revert the user's close-mode + capability choices and lose the original bootstrap date.
2. **Awareness atom** (`develop-mode-expansion.md`, `modes: [dev, simple]`, `deployStates: [deployed]`, priority 6): fires during develop flow for deployed single-slot services so the agent is prompted with the expansion command and the required plan shape. Gated on `deployed` because expansion is a post-first-deploy decision — suggesting it before the current single-slot setup has validated would be premature.
3. **Fast-path**: because `plan.IsAllExisting()` returns true for an existing runtime with no new dependencies, bootstrap auto-skips the `close` step after provision — meta write fires from the provision tail via `writeBootstrapOutputs`.

What the agent still owns: generating the import YAML fragment that creates only the new stage service (not the existing dev), appending the `setup: prod` entry to `zerops.yaml`, and running the cross-deploy `zerops_deploy sourceService="{dev}" targetService="{stage}"`. The atom body includes the step-by-step instructions; bootstrap provides the session frame and the meta merge, but does not auto-generate stage code.

**Why bootstrap, not develop**: Creating services is infrastructure work. Bootstrap already handles service creation, ServiceMeta writes, and hostname locks. Develop flow handles code changes, not infrastructure topology changes.

---

## 12. Git Foundation — source, change path, delivery, evidence

This section states ONCE the path a change takes from "where does the code come from" to
"what runs, and how do I put the previous version back". Every git-shaped rule elsewhere in
this spec (§2/§3 entry routes, §4.3 derived delivery, §4.9 deploy-from-commit + evidence,
§8 Git Lifecycle GLC-1..7, §10 launch gates) is an instance of it, and `spec-mate.md §6`
is the same model seen from the mate side. It is provider-agnostic: an origin is any HTTPS
git host reachable with a PAT (GitHub, GitLab, a self-hosted forge, anything else); the
GitHub- and GitLab-specific parts are only the two CI shapes the platform can consume today
(§12.5). Rules are numbered GF-n; a rule marked OPEN is stated so the design is whole, but
is not built — the "Home" column says where it lives once it is.

Owner intent this section serves (Karel, 2026-09-14): git is the base of every code path;
any git host; new, imported and adopted projects alike; production always means an origin
plus CI; Gitea inside Zerops is the optional default origin, not a requirement; mate
collaboration happens over a shared repository. Since 2026-10-02 the default origin inside a
Mate is its HQ, which replaced that Gitea (`spec-mate.md §10.10`, *A Mate's git*); a repository the
person keeps on GitHub is the checkout's origin instead (`spec-mate.md §5.10`).

### 12.1 Vocabulary

| Term | Meaning | Home |
|---|---|---|
| checkout | `/var/www` of ONE dev service: one working tree, one process, one subdomain. The isolation unit is the service, never a directory — worktrees are refused (`spec-mate.md §6.3`). Two agents (or two mates) on one dev service share the same uncommitted files; independent parallel work is a SECOND dev service with its own checkout. | GLC-1, `spec-mate.md §6.1/§6.3` |
| origin | The shared remote of a checkout: `ServiceMeta.RemoteURL` + `GitPushState`, written only by `git-push-setup` (probe-first, PAT as the `GIT_TOKEN` service secret, url-scoped credential helper). Optional for dev; mandatory for production (P-LP-10). | §4.3, §4.4 |
| tracked ref | The ref a target consumes — what stage or prod is built from. Recorded once in `ServiceMeta.TrackedRef` by `git-push-setup`'s confirm step (explicit `trackedRef` input, else the push source's current branch, else the remote's default branch, else `"main"`); read by the `git-push` default branch, the Actions template's `branches:` trigger, and the launch gate's remote-HEAD compare — each falling back to `"main"` independently (`trackedRefOrDefault`) when a pre-existing meta carries none. | GF-7 |
| delivery | Whatever turns a working tree or a commit into an appVersion on a target: the dev self-deploy (working tree, `-g`), deploy-from-commit (cross-deploy, §4.9), CI on push (§12.5), or the user's own CI that zcp cannot see. Which one applies is DERIVED (§4.3), never chosen by close-mode. | §4.3, §8 DM |
| evidence | Deploy result and local Work Session attempt fields (`sha`, `appVersionId`, `dirty`), subject to the correlation limitation in §4.9. Platform state owns the ACTIVE appVersion; absent evidence means source unknown. | §4.9, GF-5 |
| baseline | Adopt's starting point: repository history preserved as-is, or the repo brought to a commit-ready state WITHOUT committing the files found on the container, with the adoption-time appVersion and provenance `initialized` / `existing` stored in ServiceMeta. Neither is a claim that the tree equals the running appVersion. | GLC-7 |

### 12.2 Entry — where the source comes from

| Route | Source of code | Repo state after the route | Origin |
|---|---|---|---|
| bootstrap `recipe` / `classic` (§2) | recipe or agent-written tree in the dev service | GLC-1: repo with a reachable HEAD, identity set-if-absent. zcp seeds no ignore rules and the working tree stays uncommitted — the agent writes `.gitignore` and makes the baseline commit itself, guided (§12.6, GF-2). A buildFromGit build leaves the clone's `.git/` history in `/var/www` (observed live on the farm 2026-09-14, G2 run), so such a service adopts as `existing`; zcp does not depend on it. | none until `git-push-setup` |
| user's existing repository | `git-push-setup remoteUrl=<their host>` on the dev service; reconstruction from the remote when `/var/www/.git` is missing (§4.4) | repo synced to origin, `GitPushState=configured` | the user's, any host |
| `adopt` (§3) | a running service, with or without git | GLC-7: `initialized` (no repo / marker-only HEAD — brought to commit-ready state, working tree left uncommitted) or preserved existing HEAD; adoption appVersion and provenance recorded in metadata | none until `git-push-setup` |
| launch-production (§10) | never a clone: prod starts `startWithoutCode`, the first release is the first CI build | prod services carry no repo | the dev checkout's origin, mandatory |

### 12.3 The change path — one checkout

```
edit ──► dev self-deploy ──► commit ──► push ──► deliver to target ──► verify ──► roll back
 │            │                │          │             │                │            │
 │  mate checkpoints        working    user/agent    origin,      stage: §4.9 or    zerops_   re-activate a
 │  (refs/t3/*, turn        tree, -g,  commit with   tracked      CI-on-push        verify    recorded
 │  history; never          repo       ambient       ref          prod: CI-on-push            appVersion
 │  a delivery)             travels    identity                   only (P-LP-10/11)           (GF-8)
 └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

- **edit → dev self-deploy** is the fast loop and stays dirty by design: the working tree
  ships via zcli's stash-archive with `-g`, so the repository travels inside the artifact
  and survives the container replacement (GLC-2, verified live 2026-09-14). The recorded attempt
  for a dirty deploy carries `dirty:true` — it records HEAD plus "uncommitted changes on top",
  never "commit X is what runs". Nothing outside the repository travels with it (GF-12): the
  same self-deploy carries `notCarried`/`envFiles`/`repoState` (DM-7) so what won't survive
  the next container is visible before it's lost, not discovered after.
- **commit** is the user's or the agent's, with ambient identity (GLC-3). zcp does not
  auto-commit routine edits; its own objects (HEAD marker, adopt's commit-ready
  initialization) carry the robot identity inline but never stage or commit found files —
  the `.gitignore` and the baseline commit of what was found are the agent's, guided (GF-2).
- **push** needs an origin (§4.4). The pushed branch is the tracked ref (GF-7).
- **deliver**: a stage receives either a deploy-from-commit (§4.9, cross-deploy) or a CI
  build of the tracked ref; production receives ONLY a CI build of a pushed, clean HEAD
  (P-LP-10/11). A direct deploy on a configured pair redirects to the push only where a
  zcp-managed integration consumes pushes (§4.3).
- **verify** is `zerops_verify` against the target; **roll back** re-activates a recorded
  appVersion (GF-8) — a rebuild of an older commit is a different act and is named as such.

### 12.4 Two checkouts, one origin

`dev1` and `dev2` are two services, each its own repository (GF-1), sharing one origin.
Independent work lives on branches; integration happens at the origin (a pull request on the
host, or a fast-forward of the tracked ref); the stage consumes the tracked ref, so which
checkout produced a change is invisible to delivery. Evidence tags are written in the
checkout that deployed; the other checkout sees them only through the origin (GF-9, OPEN).
Mate checkpoints stay per checkout (`spec-mate.md §6.4`) and are never pushed.

### 12.5 Delivery contract — provider-agnostic

The contract every CI shape fulfils: **take one specific revision, deploy it to one specific
target, and leave an appVersion whose version name is that revision's sha.** Shapes:

| Shape | Status | Evidence the platform keeps |
|---|---|---|
| GitHub Actions running `zcli push` with `ZEROPS_TOKEN_PROD` (§10) / `BuildIntegration=actions` | built | version name: `BuildIntegration=actions`'s setup-aware zcli workflow (`tools/workflow_build_integration.go`) passes `--version-name "${GITHUB_REF_NAME} ${GITHUB_SHA::7}"` in a bash step (GF-10; the compact wrapper-action variant, `zeropsio/actions`, cannot express it). The §10 launch-production tag-triggered template (`tools/workflow_launch_production.go`, `ZEROPS_TOKEN_PROD`) does NOT yet — separate template, GF-10 not extended there. |
| GitLab webhook → platform pulls (`BuildIntegration=webhook`) | built | none — the appVersion DTO carries no sha (verified 2026-09-14); source is unknown to zcp unless the platform adds it |
| A Mate's HQ deploying the application's environments from its own archive of a commit | built for stages, live 2026-10-02 (`spec-mate.md §10.8`); production moves to HQ next. It replaces the Gitea Actions shape verified live 2026-09-14, retired with the Gitea; zcp writes no workflow into a Mate's repositories | version name `main <sha7>` (live: `main 48b289b`) |
| user's own CI outside zcp | supported, opaque | unknown unless it sets the version name |
| zcp-owned webhook relay | **not needed**; removed from the plan | — |

Platform limits that bound this table (verified 2026-09-14, `platform-verifier` memory):
`buildFromGit` accepts only github.com / gitlab.com; webhook receivers exist only for those
two and require the account's OAuth link; no generic authenticated build trigger carrying a
ref; no sha on the appVersion DTO; integration tokens have no TTL. Asks to the platform
team, in order of leverage: a host allowlist (or none) for `buildFromGit`; an authenticated
build trigger taking `{ref|sha, setup}`; the commit sha on the appVersion DTO; a TTL on
integration tokens. Each ask, if granted, deletes a row from the table above rather than
adding code.

### 12.6 Rules

| # | Rule | Home / status |
|---|---|---|
| GF-1 | A checkout is a service. No worktrees, no second checkout inside one service; parallel independent work is a second dev service. | `spec-mate.md §6.3` |
| GF-2 | zcp never auto-commits routine development changes. Its HEAD marker (GLC-2) and adoption initialization (GLC-7) carry inline robot identity, but never stage or commit anything the agent or user wrote. No automatic deploy or baseline tags are written. `.gitignore` and the baseline commit are the agent's, guided; zcp seeds no ignore rules. The guidance that carries this obligation to the agent renders where the obligation arises, not later: `bootstrap-close-baseline-commit` at the recipe close step and `bootstrap-adopt-baseline-commit` at the adopt provision step plus one line in the adopt transition message (adopt auto-skips close, so a close-step atom never reaches it; the repo is initialized when provision completes), `develop-self-deploy-reproducibility` in develop-active for `dev`, `simple` AND `standard` (the dev half of a pair is `Mode = standard`, so an atom scoped to `dev` alone never reaches it), and the three standard-pair stage-promotion atoms (commit the dev half over SSH before the cross-deploy; `sha="<commit>"` ships an exact commit without touching the tree; rollback is `appVersion=<id>`). Proven by the farm oracles, not by the prompt — by CONTENT, never by author, because a dev container's ambient identity is the robot's until git-push-setup derives a human one (GLC-3): G1 requires a non-empty HEAD tree (zcp's marker is the empty tree) and G6 a tracked cargo file (zcp stages nothing), both a tracked `.gitignore` and no `.env`/`node_modules` in HEAD; G3's prompt names no parameter and its `toolArg` row requires `sha~` on the stage deploy (`spec-scenarios.md §9.3`). | GLC-2/3/7, §4.9 |
| GF-3 | Deploy-from-commit is a cross-deploy only. A self-deploy always ships the working tree with `.git` (`-g`); shipping an extracted tree to the source service itself would delete the container's repository. | §4.9; `TestDeploySSH_ShaSelfTarget_Refused` |
| GF-4 | The `zerops.yaml` that is validated is the one that is deployed: the commit's for a sha deploy, the working tree's otherwise. | §4.9 |
| GF-5 | Evidence never overclaims. No source evidence ⇒ "source unknown"; uncommitted working-tree changes ⇒ `dirty:true`; a baseline ⇒ `initialized` or `existing`, never parity with the running appVersion; the platform's active appVersion is the only "what runs". | §4.9, GLC-7 |
| GF-6 | Production is delivered only by CI from an origin, from a clean, pushed HEAD; zcp never self-deploys production. | P-LP-10/11 — unchanged |
| GF-7 | The tracked ref is recorded once per target and read by the push default, the CI template and the launch gate. A meta that carries NO tracked ref (every ServiceMeta written before this rule) is compared by the launch and release push-proof against `HEAD` — the remote's default branch, exactly the pre-GF-7 compare — never against the literal `main` (`tools.launchPushProofRef`; `TestLaunchGate_LegacyMetaWithoutTrackedRef_ComparesHEAD`, `TestHandleRelease_LegacyMetaWithoutTrackedRef_ComparesHEAD`); a recorded ref is never softened. | built — `ServiceMeta.TrackedRef`, `ops/git.CurrentBranch` (`TestCurrentBranch_*`), `tools.resolveContainerTrackedRef`/`localGitBranchDetector` (`TestGitPushSetupContainer_RecordsTrackedRef_*`, `TestGitPushSetupLocal_RecordsTrackedRef_*`), `tools.resolveTrackedBranch`/`trackedRefOrDefault` (`TestGitPush_DefaultBranchFromMeta`), Actions template (`TestBuildIntegration_ActionsTemplateUsesTrackedRef`), launch gate (`TestLaunchGate_ComparesTrackedRef`) |
| GF-8 | Rollback is the re-activation of a recorded appVersion (`PUT /app-version/{id}/deploy`, the R2 path generalised from `latest` to any recorded id) with no build; deploying an older commit is a NEW build and is offered as the fallback, named as such. The candidate ids come from `zerops_events` or the status envelope's `rollback` block (`ops.AppVersionCandidates`), never from a probe deploy call with a fake id. | built — `ops.ReactivateAppVersion` (`TestReactivateAppVersion_*`), `ops.AppVersionCandidates` (`TestAppVersionCandidates_*`), `tools.runAppVersionRollback` (`TestDeploySSH_AppVersionID_*`) |
| GF-9 | Git tags are user-owned release markers, not a deploy ledger. No automatic deploy/baseline tag creation, lookup or synchronization. | §4.9, GLC-7 |
| GF-10 | Every zcp-driven build passes `--version-name`, so `SearchAppVersions.name` is a platform-side breadcrumb independent of local session history, read back by the Mate app and HQ as the commit a service runs. Every name is exactly two tokens, a label for people and the commit's first 7 hex characters, which both readers take for the commit whatever the label says: a working-tree deploy is labelled by its branch — `main 7e2d4c1` — or `HEAD` when HEAD is on no branch (detached; git refuses a branch named HEAD), with `-dirty` on the sha when uncommitted changes ride on top (`main 7e2d4c1-dirty`), which every reader takes for no commit; a deploy-from-commit is labelled by the checked-out branch when the commit is its tip, else `commit` (`commit 7e2d4c1`), never dirty; the Actions template names the pushed branch, `${GITHUB_REF_NAME} ${GITHUB_SHA::7}` in a bash step; no reachable HEAD ⇒ no flag. Never itself proof of what was deployed (GF-5) — correlate with the resulting appVersion. | built for zcp-driven SSH/local deploys and the `BuildIntegration=actions` template — `ops.versionNameFor`, `ops.commitVersionName`, `git.HeadStatus` (the branch in the same round trip), `DeployResult.VersionName` (`TestBuildSSHCommand_VersionNameFromHead`, `TestVersionName`, `TestCommitVersionName`, `TestHeadStatus_Branch`, `TestReadSelfDeployPreflight_Branch`, `TestDeployLocal_VersionNameFromHead`), Actions template's `--version-name "${GITHUB_REF_NAME} ${GITHUB_SHA::7}"` (`TestActionsTemplate_VersionNameSHA`) — OPEN: the §10 launch-production tag-triggered template (§12.5) still doesn't |
| GF-11 | A rejected push is the user's decision: zcp classifies (rebase/merge/replace-remote) and never force-pushes or merges on its own. A non-fast-forward rejection returns `GIT_PUSH_NON_FAST_FORWARD` with the tracked ref's `remoteAhead`/`localAhead`/`unrelated` divergence (`ops.ProbeGitDivergence`) and a `next` block naming exactly the three options; `git-push-setup`'s confirm-mode success result surfaces the same classification proactively as `remote: {ref, state}` (`ahead`/`diverged`/`unrelated` ⇒ `remoteStateWarning`) so the risk is visible before the first push, not only after a rejected one. | built — `ops.IsNonFastForwardRejection`/`ProbeGitDivergence`/`ClassifyRemoteRefState` (`TestProbeGitDivergence_*`, `TestClassifyRemoteRefState`), `tools.classifyGitPushNonFastForward`/`newGitPushNonFastForwardError` in `handleGitPush`/`handleLocalGitPush` (`TestGitPush_NonFastForward_Classified`, `TestGitPush_NonFastForward_NeverForces`, `TestConvertError_NonFastForward`), `tools.probeGitPushRemoteState` in `confirmGitPushSetupContainer`/`confirmGitPushSetupLocal` (`TestGitPushSetup_RemoteState_Empty/InSync/Ahead/Unrelated`) |
| GF-12 | Owner principle (Karel, 2026-09-15): a dev checkout is reproducible from its repository and nothing else on it is persistent — code in git, configuration in service env vars, data in managed services, no `.env` files. The self-deploy boundary is exactly what zcli's git archiver ships (see the zcli self-deploy archiver facts under DM-7/DM-8): a self-deploy's pre-flight read and its two hard refusals are how zcp makes that boundary visible instead of silent. | built — DM-7/DM-8, `git.ReadSelfDeployPreflight` (`TestReadSelfDeployPreflight_*`), `TestDeploySSH_SelfDeploy_PreflightNotCarried`, `TestDeploySSH_GitFile_Refused`, `TestDeploySSH_Submodules_Refused` |
| GF-13 | A `strategy="git-push"` records an in-flight `DeployAttempt` (no `SucceededAt`) under the push source only when something can later resolve it — a wired `BuildIntegration=webhook\|actions` build watch (container path) or its manual `record-deploy` fallback (both paths; the local path has no build watch of its own, so the fallback is its only resolver). Nothing was transmitted (`NOTHING_TO_PUSH`), the destination is this Mate's HQ (container path only — nothing ever builds off a Mate's change branch, `spec-mate.md §10.10`; a local git-push never targets a Mate's HQ, there is no Mate on a developer's own machine), or `BuildIntegration` is `none`/unset: none of these has a resolver, so recording the placeholder there left a permanent, unexplained `Success:false` "failed deploy" on the push source (`deployAttemptsToInfo` renders any empty `SucceededAt` as a failure) — never healed, so `needsDeploy`/auto-close stayed stuck on a service that never actually failed. A push that no build follows is delivery, not a deploy of that service, so those outcomes record nothing. A genuine push failure (pre-flight, transport, non-fast-forward) carries `Error`+`FailureClass`, but is recorded under the SAME trackability gate as a success, never unconditionally: a destination nothing can ever resolve records neither outcome, since a recorded failure there would be exactly as permanent and unexplained as the in-flight placeholder this gate exists to prevent — nothing would ever record a superseding success either. The error itself always still reaches the agent in the `CallToolResult`; only the `DeployAttempt` write is skipped. `NOTHING_TO_PUSH` detection itself was broken on the local path — `git push`'s status line lands on stderr, and the shared `runGitWithEnv` only returns stdout — so a real up-to-date local push always read as `PUSHED`, never exercising this gate's NOTHING_TO_PUSH branch at all; fixed by a push-only `runGitPushCapture` that reads both streams, without widening `runGitWithEnv` for its other callers. | built — container: `tools.gitPushBuildIntegrationConfigured`, `tools.gitPushDestinationTrackable` (success AND failure share it — the `trackable` local at the success gate, and the `recordAttempt` closure at every failure point in `handleGitPush`) (`TestDeployTool_GitPush_NoFutureBuild_NeverRecordsDanglingAttempt`, `TestDeployTool_GitPush_WebhookIntegration_StillRecordsInFlightAttempt`, `TestDeployTool_GitPush_UntrackableDestination_FailureRecordsNoDanglingAttempt`, `TestDeployTool_GitPush_TrackableDestination_FailureStillRecordsAttempt`); local: `tools.localGitPushTrackable` + `tools.runGitPushCapture` in `handleLocalGitPush`, and the same `gitPushBuildIntegrationConfigured` gate on the `record` closure's failure side (`TestHandleLocalGitPush_NoFutureBuild_NeverRecordsDanglingAttempt`, `TestHandleLocalGitPush_NoFutureBuild_FailureRecordsNoDanglingAttemptEither`, `TestLocalGitPushTrackable`, `TestHandleLocalGitPush_NothingToPush_DetectedFromStderr`, `TestHandleLocalGitPush_WebhookIntegration_StillRecordsInFlightAttempt`) |

### 12.7 Open items and owners

- GF-10 platform-side source evidence — the `--version-name` breadcrumb is built for SSH/local deploys and the `BuildIntegration=actions` template; the §10 launch-production tag-triggered template (`tools/workflow_launch_production.go`) still doesn't pass one, and reading the breadcrumb back via `SearchAppVersions` to correlate a push with its resulting appVersion is not built at all — needs a caller that reads it and treats the mapping as evidence, never proof (GF-5).
- Mate commit/push through zcp (`spec-mate.md §6.3`) — the fork's side of GF-2.
- Platform asks (§12.5) — owner sends; each granted ask removes code.

---

## 13. Pool Migration Flow

`zerops_workflow workflow="pool-migrate" service=<dev>` (also accepted with `action="start"`) is a
stateless multi-call narrowing, the §10 pattern, that walks one part whose dev environment is a
plain dev service — a dev-only service, the dev half of a variant-A pair, or the dev services of a
variant-B pair, whose repository lives on a code volume (PM-13) — onto a dev pool (§8 DP); a dev serving several parts moves each part onto a pool of its own, one part per call, and
is retired once every part has moved (PM-12). Each call reads live state, names the part's current step and the next exact
call, and carries the guidance of the `pool-migrate-active` atoms. The flow owns two mutations of
its own, the env copy and the retire; every other step runs through a tool that already carries
its gates (`zerops_import`, `zerops_deploy`, the agent's own git, `zerops_lease`,
`zerops_dev_server`, `zerops_verify`). Its read side writes nothing anywhere.

### 13.1 Steps

| Step | Derived from | Next call |
|---|---|---|
| `provision` | the pool's hostname absent from the direct service list; or the pool listed with a live process of its own that is not a build (§3.5) | `zerops_import` with the rendered stanza in `next.args.content`; while the pool's processes run, `zerops_process action="wait" service=<pool>` |
| `env` | the pool listed and settled, and its service env not yet the dev's (PM-4: a key to write, or the pool's default repository not the dev's origin) | `zerops_workflow workflow="pool-migrate" action="pool-env" service=<dev> pool=<pool>` |
| `image` | the pool settled and its env the dev's, and the pool not yet running an app version of the pool setup in the dev checkout's HEAD commit (PM-11); or a build of the pool running | `zerops_deploy sourceService=<dev> targetService=<pool> setup=<pool> sha=<commit>` once the commit passes every check (PM-11), else none — `nextUnavailable` names what to fix and commit; while a build of the pool runs, `zerops_process action="wait" service=<pool>` |
| `move` | the image step done (PM-11), and zcp holds no lease of the pool on the work's branch while the census names work that holds the retire (PM-6), or no work's branch is named | none — the agent's own git; `nextUnavailable` names what to push, and the lease for once it is moved |
| `lease` | the image step done, nothing left that holds the retire, and zcp's records name no lease of the pool on the work's branch of the dev's origin | `zerops_lease action="acquire" pool=<pool> branch=<work's branch>` |
| `ready-to-retire` | the image step done and zcp's records name a lease of the pool on the work's branch of the dev's origin (`ops.PoolMigrateLeases`) | `zerops_workflow workflow="pool-migrate" action="retire" service=<dev> pool=<pool>` — its first call diagnoses (PM-8), and its `why` names what the retire deletes as the diagnosis does (for a variant-B pair every dev service and, last, the code volume, PM-13); for a dev serving several parts, the retire `service=<dev>` once every part is here, else the flow call walking the first part that is not (PM-12) |
| `retiring` | a retire record in flight (PM-7) — from it alone, before any other read | the same retire call, which resumes it |
| `retired` | a retire record `retired`, its dev hostname not held by a service with another id | none |

The move step's content rides every step's answer from the first call on: `census`, the dev
service's unsaved work (PM-2), and `workBranch`, the branch a lease of the pool checks out. The
agent may move work at any step; the flow commits and pushes nothing (GF-2).

The pool stanza names one service: hostname `<stem>pool` (the dev hostname without its `dev`
suffix; `pool=<hostname>` overrides it, on every call; a hostname the platform would refuse is
`pool-hostname-invalid`, one a non-pool service holds — or a pool another pair holds, PM-9 — is `pool-hostname-taken`), type `swarm@1`,
`minContainers: 0`, `maxContainers: 3`, the dev service's own `verticalAutoscaling` (CPU mode and
the CPU, RAM and disk bounds, read by id), and `vault.sshIsolation: "vpn service@<zcp hostname>"`.
The pool's own env rides `vault`: a service-level `envVariables` block is deprecated in the import
schema (it still creates the service's env, every value plain — `spec-zerops-env-lifecycle.md`
§8b), and `sshIsolation` reaches a pool's containers only as a service env variable (§8 DP). The
stanza never carries a credential — `GIT_TOKEN` reaches the pool in the env step.

A response carries `status` (`step` | `not-a-candidate`; on the retire's own answers also
`retire-refused` | `aborted`), `service` (the pair's dev half when the
call named its stage), `stages`, `pool`, for a dev serving several parts `part` (the stage of the
part the answer walks) and `parts` (`[{stage, setup, pool, step}]`, PM-12), `step`, `steps` (each `done` | `current` | `pending`),
`next` `{tool, args, why}` or `nextUnavailable`, `census` and `workBranch` `{branch, source}` or
`{missing}` on every step, `env` (PM-4) and `envRequests` once the pool is settled, `image`
(PM-11) once the pool holds the dev's env, `retire` (the record's `phase`, `devServiceId`,
`forced`, `bundle` `{path, sha256, bytes, refs, stashes, reflogTips, trees}`, `restore`,
`keptInBundle`, `notKept`, `abortReason`, for a variant-B pair `codeVolume` `{hostname, serviceId,
mountPath, refused, bundle, restore}`, and `gaps` once retired) once a retire started, `action`
(`pool-env` on the env step's own answer, `retire` on the retire's), `refusals[]` `{reason,
message, recovery?}` (on a variant-B retire's `retiring` answer too: what holds the volume's
delete, PM-13), `warnings`, `guidance` (the `pool-migrate-plan` atom, on every step the
`pool-migrate-move` atom, once the pool is settled the `pool-migrate-env` atom, once the pool holds
the dev's env the `pool-migrate-image` atom, and once the image step is done — and on every answer
from a retire record — the `pool-migrate-retire` atom) and `inputs` (what the next call passes
again).

### 13.2 Invariants

| ID | Invariant |
|----|-----------|
| PM-1 | Flow shape. `workflow="pool-migrate"`, with or without `action="start"`, is read-side and writes nothing: no state file, meta, env, ref or crew save. Its SSH calls are the census on the dev service (PM-2; a variant-B pair's on the code volume's checkout through the dev's mount, PM-13) and, once the pool holds the dev's env, the image step's read-only read of the dev checkout's HEAD commit (PM-11); its platform calls are the direct reads `ListServicesDirect` and `GetProjectProcessesDirect`, the dev's by-id `GetService`, and once the pool is settled the service env of the dev, the pool and the stages with each one's by-id `GetService` and active app-version env (PM-4: it reads no secret, and zcp's own records only by reading). The step is derived from those direct reads, never an ES search (§3.5), so a call repeated after compaction lands on the same step (P4). Beside those it reads only local files: zcp's own lease records, for the lease step (PM-6), and the retire's record — a part whose retire started is answered from that record before any other read (PM-7). Its phase is `pool-migrate-active` and `BuildPlan` returns an empty Plan for it; the typed tool output stays nil. `TestPoolMigrate_ReadSideWritesNothing`, `TestPoolMigrate_StepFromLiveState_NoPool_Provision`, `TestPoolMigrate_StepFromLiveState_PoolPresent_Env`, `TestPoolMigrate_StepFromLiveState_PoolImportInFlight_Provision`, `TestPoolMigrate_StepFromLiveState_DecidesOnDirectReads`, `TestPoolMigrate_RepeatCall_SameStep`, `TestPoolMigrate_Dispatch_ReachesTheFlow`, `TestPoolMigrate_Integration_PairNamesProvisionWithAStanza`, `TestPoolMigrate_ReadSide_LeaseAndRetireSteps`. |
| PM-2 | Unsaved-work census. Every candidate step reads `census` in one SSH round trip of read-only git over the dev service's repository at `/var/www` (a variant-B pair's at the code volume's mount, PM-13) — the main checkout and every linked worktree, `.crew/*` included (`ops.ReadPoolMigrateCensus`). Per tree (`trees[]`, the main checkout first): its HEAD and branch, the branch's upstream `{remote, branch}`, a detached HEAD and the commits it alone holds (`detachedCommits`: on no branch and no remote), a registered worktree whose directory is gone (`missing`), what it is in the middle of (`inProgress`: merging, rebasing, cherry-picking, reverting, bisecting), and from `git status --porcelain=v2` its `staged`, `modified`, `conflicted` and `untracked` paths (an untracked directory named once), its `.env`/`.env.*` files git does not carry (`envFiles`: untracked or ignored; one inside a wholly ignored directory rides that directory's entry) and an `ignored` summary (a wholly ignored directory named once). Repository-wide: `branches[]`, each local branch holding commits on no remote branch, with the count; `stashes[]`, every entry `{ref, commit, message}`; `reflogOnly`, the commits only a reflog reaches (reset, amended or rebased away — every reflog, every worktree's HEAD included, minus every stash entry), counted, with the newest ten; `t3Refs`, the refs under `refs/t3/` counted by namespace. Each path list is `{count, paths}`, the first ten named. `clean` is true when it names no unsaved work: no staged, modified, conflicted or untracked path, nothing in progress, no `.env` file, no detached commit, no branch commit on no remote, no stash entry, no reflog-only commit and no submodule — a repository of its own, its objects in `.git/modules` (or its own `.git`), whose work the census cannot count and the bundle does not carry: a commit or a stash inside one leaves the superproject's status clean, so `submodules` names each tree's gitlinks (its index's mode-160000 entries) and a submodule's status entry is named there, never as a staged or modified path; `t3Refs` and `ignored` never make it unclean (Mate's own refs; files a checkout reproduces from its repository, GF-12). The census writes nothing — no ref, object, reflog, lock or index: every git command in it only reads, and `git status` runs under `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0`, so it never refreshes and rewrites an index whose entries are stale, as a plain `git status` does. A step that fails, or an answer cut short, is no census, never a clean one: the flow refuses `dev-unreadable` naming the step and git's stderr — and so is a worktree whose path holds a newline, which `git worktree list --porcelain` prints verbatim and a line-by-line read would cut, taking the worktree for one whose directory is gone (the `-z` listing finds it; the refusal says to move that worktree). Crew worktrees (PM-9) are read from it. `workBranch` is the branch a lease checks out: in a Mate the open change's branch (`source: change`), without one the dev checkout's own branch (`current`) unless it is `main` or a `mate/` branch, which HQ takes no push to; elsewhere the dev checkout's branch's upstream when it is on origin (`upstream`); otherwise `missing` says what names one (a detached checkout, no upstream, an upstream on another remote, a branch HQ refuses). `TestPoolMigrateCensus_EveryPlaceWorkHides_Named`, `TestPoolMigrateCensus_CleanRepo_Clean`, `TestPoolMigrateCensus_EachItemMakesItUnclean`, `TestPoolMigrateCensus_WritesNothing`, `TestPoolMigrateCensus_OneRoundTrip`, `TestPoolMigrateCensus_ScriptRunsOnlyReadOnlyGit`, `TestPoolMigrateCensus_NotARepository_Error`, `TestParsePoolMigrateCensus_IncompleteOrFailed_Error`, `TestPoolMigrate_Census_Rendered`, `TestPoolMigrate_Census_CleanDevSaysSo`, `TestPoolMigrate_WorkBranch_FromChangeCurrentOrUpstream`, `TestPoolMigrateCensus_Submodule_NamedNeverClean`, `TestPoolMigrateCensus_WorktreePathWithNewline_NoCensus`. |

One SSH round trip builds it on the dev service, in `/tmp/zcp-pool-migrate` — outside the repository (GF-2), removed before and after: a `git clone --mirror --local --no-hardlinks` of `/var/www` — its objects directory copied whole, so the objects no ref names come along, and nothing hard-linked: a repository on a code volume is a filesystem of its own (PM-13), where a hard link from `/tmp` fails and a clone told `--local` dies on it (`Invalid cross-device link`, rc 128; live-verified 2026-10-24) — with a ref under `refs/zcp-migrate/` for every place work hides that no ref names — each stash entry (`stash/<n>`; a plain `--all` bundle carries only `stash@{0}`), each commit a reflog mentions, every worktree's HEAD reflog and every stash entry included (`reflog/<sha>`, `git rev-list --no-walk --reflog`), and per tree in `git worktree list` order its HEAD (`trees/<i>/head`), a WIP commit of its index and tracked files (`trees/<i>/wip`, `git stash create` against a copy of the index — a plain one rewrites the index file) and a commit of its untracked files (`trees/<i>/untracked`, written through an index of the scratch directory's own; never a `.env`/`.env.*` file, an ignored file, or an untracked repository of its own, which `bundle.nested` names; a submodule's repository is never carried either, PM-6) — each created before the clone so the mirror holds its objects; then `fsck` of the mirror, and the fingerprint (PM-5) read before and after, which must agree. `git bundle create - --all` of the mirror streams over SSH stdout alone (`ops.SSHStreamer`, implemented by `platform.SystemSSHDeployer.StreamSSH`: stdout to the file as it arrives, stderr apart, no tty, never `CombinedOutput`) into a `.part` file that is verified on this container — `git bundle verify`, a fetch of every ref into a scratch repository, its refs exactly the mirror's, `fsck` — before it is renamed into place. | PM-4 | The env step. `action="pool-env"` (`workflow="pool-migrate"` or none; any other workflow is refused) re-runs every check of the read side and, on a settled pool only, gives the pool the dev service's own service env (`ops.ApplyPoolMigrateEnv`); before that it writes nothing and says so. What is copied: the dev's user-set layer — its slim service env minus the keys its active app-version's zerops.yaml owns (`FetchServiceUserEnvs`; those reach the pool with the pool setup in the image step) — never project env, which every service reads already. A plain value is copied as the API reads it, `${...}` references included. A secret — a row the platform holds sensitive, or one it masks from zcp's token — is read in the dev container's environment over SSH, never through the API (OD-10: a Mate's own key reads every sensitive value `REDACTED` on every API path, and can write them — live-verified 2026-10-23): one fresh session, every key a single-quoted word handed to `printenv` with its stderr discarded, the keys riding the positional parameters and never a shell variable — one sharing a key's name (a loop over `k`) would change that exported value and read the script's — the answer NUL-framed between markers so no value is word-split, expanded or echoed into stderr, and the script always exits 0; the SSH error is never wrapped and its output is dropped (`ops.PoolEnvReadError` names host and exit only). zcp writes it with its own key, `sensitive:true`, through the hand-rolled userData writer (`platform.CreateServiceEnvVar`); a plain key keeps the dev's flag. | PM-5 | Fence, locks, fingerprint. From the fence through the delete the retire holds both pair locks — the dev's and the pool's (`workflow.LockPair`, in a fixed order) — so no HQ pass, stand-up or describe of zcp runs git on the pair; it takes the dev's mount on this container down first (`fenced`), and a mount it cannot take down stops the retire. The fingerprint (`ops.ReadPoolMigrateFingerprint`, one SSH round trip writing nothing but unreferenced objects) reduces the repository to digests: every ref, every commit a reflog mentions, and per tree its HEAD, the blob ids of its `git status --porcelain=v2` and `git ls-files -s` — never the index file's bytes or mtime — the trees of its working files and index as `git stash create` sees them, and the tree of its untracked files. It is taken with the bundle and read again right before the delete, the dev fenced again first; anything that moved aborts the retire (`fingerprint-changed`, naming the refs, the reflogs or each checkout that changed, or a worktree added or removed), and so does a fingerprint that cannot be read (`fingerprint-unreadable`). zcp cannot command the Mate app's own writers; the window left is the one delete call. `TestPoolMigrateRetire_FenceBeforeBundle`, `TestPoolMigrateRetire_FingerprintChanged_Aborts`, `TestPoolMigrateRetire_UnmountFails_AbortsAndRestores`, `TestPoolMigrateFingerprint_UnchangedEqual_EachChangeNamed`. |
| PM-6 | The retire. `action="retire"` (`workflow="pool-migrate"` or none; any other workflow is refused) re-runs every check of the read side, then the retire's guards — each a direct read — naming every one that fails (`status: "retire-refused"`, nothing written): the step reached (`move` with a work's branch, `lease` or `ready-to-retire`, else `not-ready`); the dev read by id — the id the delete uses — and not DELETING (`dev-deleting`) or gone (`dev-not-live`); no live process referencing it (`dev-busy`, §3.5); no active launch-production whose target names the dev, a stage, or no runtime (`launch-active`); no project or service env variable but the dev's own holding `${<dev>_` (`env-reference`; values zcp's token reads masked are named in a warning, a pool's or Box's own credential never); no custom-domain intent and no public routing to the dev's id (`domain`); and a lease zcp holds of the pool on the work's branch of the dev's origin — its record (`ops.PoolMigrateLeases`), the pool's orchestrator listing it held (`ops.LeaseList`, which renews it, DP-2; no orchestrator wired, or one that does not answer whether the lease is still zcp's (`unverified`), is `lease-unreadable` — the lease never reached over ssh — and one no longer held `no-lease`) — passing verify as `zerops_verify` verifies a lease (`lease-unverified`), with no waiver. Every part the dev serves has its pool: for a dev serving several parts every part's step and lease are guards of their own (PM-12). Then the acknowledgment (PM-8), then the unsaved work: every census item (PM-2) but the commits only a reflog reaches holds the retire (`unsaved-work`) unless the acknowledged call carries `force=true` — a submodule among them, the refusal saying to push each one's commits, branches and stashes to its own remote, and a forced retire naming it in `notKept`, never in `keptInBundle`, since the bundle carries no submodule's repository — the bundle carries every reflog tip as a ref, so an amend, a reset or a rebase never needs force, and those commits are named in `keptInBundle`. Under both pair locks — the record re-read under them, so a retire another call started meanwhile answers from its record and is never started twice — the record `.zcp/state/migrate/<dev>.json` (mode 0600) is written: the dev's id from the by-id read, the pool's id, the pool setup, the stages, the origin, `forced`. The phases run, each saved before the work it names: `started`; `fenced` (PM-5); `bundled` — the final census (crew worktrees, or unsaved work that appeared since on an unforced retire, abort), the bundle (PM-3), `keptInBundle` and `notKept`; `rewritten` — `workflow.MigratePairToPool` (PM-10); `deleting` — re-fence, the fingerprint re-checked equal, then `ops.DeleteByID` with the recorded id, never a hostname resolved through the lagging search, and its process polled, then the dev read by id while it reads DELETING (it lingers a few seconds after the process; a deleted service's by-id read answers HTTP 400 `serviceStackNotFound`, not only a 404 — `ops.IsServiceGone`); `retired` once the dev reads gone by id — its HQ backoff record dropped, any stale mount cleared. A dev still DELETING, or still live after its delete, keeps the record at `deleting`, and the answer (`retiring`) says call again. | PM-7 | Resume and abort. Every later retire call acts on the record and the recorded id, never a fresh name lookup, deciding from the dev's state by id: DELETING keeps the record (`retiring`: call again); gone finishes whatever the phase — the pair re-keyed onto the pool now when the dev went outside zcp before the re-key, so the stage never stays under a dead key — and marks it `retired`; live resumes from the phase on the acknowledged call, under both pair locks with the record re-read under them (a record another call moved answers as it is now): from `started`, `fenced` or `bundled` the guards run again — a refusal now aborts — and the dev is re-fenced and the phase done again (a new bundle: `bundled` is saved before its bundle and the bundle recorded only once written and verified, so a record cut there holds none); from `rewritten` whose re-key never began — the phase is saved before its work, so a cut there leaves the pair keyed by the dev and no pair of a recorded pool moved from it — the guards run again as from `bundled`, still before the split (PM-12), a refusal now aborting; from `rewritten` past that the re-key is finished (a no-op once done) and the retire goes on to `deleting`; from `deleting` it re-fences, re-checks the fingerprint and deletes by the recorded id again. A service that later takes the old hostname has another id and is never touched; a retire of that service starts a record of its own — under the pair locks too, the retired record of the old id answers nothing for it — and the old record and its bundle, the only copy of the old dev's work, are kept apart as `.zcp/state/migrate/<dev>.<old id>.json` and `.bundle` — and a variant-B retire's second bundle as `.volume.bundle`, the newer and only copy of the late writes (PM-13) — each restore list naming its bundle there, never written over. Absence from a search is never taken as gone. `aborted` is the end of a refusal or a failure after `started` while the dev reads live: the re-key reversed by `workflow.RevertPoolPair` (applied to what the pair's file holds now; nothing to reverse when the pair is still keyed by the dev), the mount put back, the record marked `aborted` with its reason, the bundle kept; a new retire starts a new record. | PM-10 | The pool-keyed pair. After a retire the pair's one meta (§8 E8) is keyed by the pool and marked `devPool` (`ServiceMeta.DevIsPool()`; `ServiceSnapshot.DevIsPool` for pure readers, off the wire). `workflow.MigratePairToPool` re-keys inside one `.services.lock` — the pool-keyed file is written, then the dev-keyed one removed, so a cut leaves both and the next call finishes the move; a move already done is a no-op. It carries the stages, every lifecycle stamp, the HQ record and the stage's own records, moves the dev's public-access intent under the pool's key with its stamp cleared, takes the pool's setup as `primarySetupName`, and sets the dev container's own facts — its setup, adopt-time baseline, serves-HTTP and public-access records and the fingerprint of the credential it held — aside under `devPool.migratedFrom`, which nothing reads as the pool's (the next HQ pass renews the pool's credential, taking over what DP-16's per-pool record knows of it). A variant-B pair's `codeVolume` rides the pool-keyed pair — for a split, the first part's alone, so each dev service resolves to one pair — until the retire has deleted the volume, and leaves it then (PM-13). It refuses simple and local modes, an unfinished bootstrap, a pair already keyed by a pool, a pair with several stages (each part takes a pool of its own, `workflow.MigratePairToPools`, PM-12) and a pool hostname another pair names, changing nothing. `workflow.RevertPoolPair`, for an aborted retire, renames the file back and applies the inverse to what it holds now: writes since the move stay, the dev's own facts come back from `migratedFrom`, and nothing is restored from a snapshot. `devPool.serviceId` keeps the pool service's id beside the HQ record's credential fingerprint — the two facts DP-16's per-pool record keeps, which the pair's record takes over (below). Readers: `ModeFor` projects no mode onto the pool half, so no mode-sensitive guidance or probe applies to it, and only an atom about pools binds to its snapshot; the pool's snapshot keys the pair on the envelope — `stages[]` (today `stageHostname`), the HQ record, and a status line naming the pair; `BuildPlan` never plans the pool half, and the stage's deploy runs from a held lease of the pool (`sourceService=<alias>`, a cross-deploy whatever the git-push state, since a git-push from a lease is refused — DP-6), else its step is `zerops_lease action="acquire" pool=<pool>` — never the pool; develop's start reads the leases as `action="status"` does (every envelope starts from the same lease reader, `tools.leaseEnvelopeOptions`), so the two name the same step in one session, and the auto-close line names that call too; deploy targets drop a hostname no pair names (the retired dev, in any process's session — develop admits only hostnames a pair names) and the pool half. The HQ and adopt readers ask `DevIsPool` before any container work on the meta's hostname, which reaches a random container of the pool (§8 PS-3): the repository pass renews the Mate credential on the pool — in a Mate only, as DP-16 — by `devPool.serviceId`, over no ssh, and points no checkout origin and syncs no `main` (a pool has no checkout of its own; each lease checks its branch out from the repository); before renewing it takes DP-16's per-pool record over when that record names `devPool.serviceId` — the pair's record adopts its fingerprint when the pair names none yet (the re-key cleared it) or it is the enrollment's, and the per-pool record goes, so the per-pool renewal never renews the pool a second time (an acquire that finds no record writes once more and records it again for the next pass); the pass never wires such a pair — not when git-push reads unconfigured, nor in another application HQ moved the Mate to, which it says, asking HQ for no repository; every HQ record write of a pass skips a pair gone from disk meanwhile instead of leaving a stub under the hostname it read (`workflow.ErrSkipWrite`), so a pass in flight across the re-key never brings the retired dev back as a pair; status reads no checkout origin on the pool and develop's start names no checkout of it; `git-push-setup` refuses either hostname of such a pair before any ssh call or env write, pointing at a lease of the pool (zcp's own wiring path refuses alike); the adopt git-push reconcile reads nothing there; the group recipe carries the part as the dev service it was moved from — `migratedFrom`'s hostname and setup, its type, scale, public access and variables read from its first live stage, never the pool's, and no zerops.yaml read under the pool's hostname — so a Mate made from the recipe gets a dev service again, and leaves out and says a pair with no `migratedFrom` or no live stage, or one wired in another application. The delivery and launch readers ask it too, before any ssh: `release` and git-push (`zerops_deploy strategy="git-push"`) refuse either hostname by name (`INVALID_USAGE`), pointing at a lease of the pool, which pushes its branch and its tags itself (§8 DP-6); build-integration reads no origin on the pool, its `gh secret set` commands take the PAT the person gives instead of reading `GIT_TOKEN` over ssh from the pool, and its CI builds every stage with the stage's setup — never the pool, which no push rebuilds (a `buildTarget` naming the pool is no override); verify and record-deploy redirect nothing off either half; the push-delivery redirect never fires for such a pair — the stage's deploy from a lease is its delivery — and no divergence warning names a push from the pool; develop's runtime list leaves the pool out, and a scope naming it is refused by name before any other answer about the runtimes, pointing at the stage and a lease; a lease's PA-1 stamp lands on the pool's record (the dev's intent moved there), the stage's record untouched; launch refuses the pair by name before any read (§10 P-LP-17); the Mate stage rebuild reads nothing under the pool's hostname on the mount. `TestNoContainerWorkOnPoolDevHalf` pins the rule across `internal/tools`: it follows a meta's Hostname — off the meta, through locals, returned by a package function, or in an untagged field of a package struct a literal sets from it — into ssh, a mount, a local read under a mount root, a rendered ssh command line or a package or ops function that hands it on, and requires the function that obtains the meta to ask `DevIsPool` (a helper handed the meta passes the question to its callers). The retire (PM-6) is the only writer. `TestMigratePairToPool_KeysThePairOnThePool`, `TestMigratePairToPool_RenamesTheFileInOneLock`, `TestMigratePairToPool_NotAMovablePair_Refused`, | PM-11 | The image step. Once the pool holds the dev's env (PM-4 `env.done`) the step is `image`, and every answer carries `image` (`ops.ReadPoolMigrateImage`): the pool setup — the zerops.yaml setup named after the pool, the one a deploy to the pool builds — read in the dev checkout's HEAD commit (the census's main tree), never its working tree or the mount, in one SSH round trip of read-only git (`git cat-file`, `git log`, optional locks off). It reads the config file a deploy of the commit reads (`zerops.yaml`, else `zerops.yml`) and `.zcp/dev-servers.json`, each byte for byte and framed by its size, and the committer date of the commit's last change of the config file; a read cut short or failed is never an empty commit: the step stays `image` and names no deploy, its `nextUnavailable` naming git's stderr — on the env step's own answer too — and a checkout with no commit is checked without SSH. The files are parsed by the parsers every other reader uses (`ops.ParseZeropsYmlContent`, `extends` resolved; `devsup.ParseDeclaration`). `image.missing` names each check the commit fails, each message saying what to change, and while it names any the step names no deploy (`nextUnavailable`, which also names the setup's files the checkout changed since the commit, `image.uncommitted`, as far as the census lists them): `commit` (no commit, or one the repository no longer holds); `config-file` (none, or it does not parse); `pool-setup` (no setup named after the pool, the setups listed); `start` (`run.start` is not `zcp-devsup run`); `install` (no `run.prepareCommands` item downloading `zcp-devsup-linux-amd64` — the line `AdoptSupervisorYAML` recognizes; the message names the dev setup's own line when it carries one); `preview-port-base` (no declaration, one that does not parse, no `previewPortBase`, or a last preview port past 65435); `preview-ports` (each of `previewPortBase+1` … `previewPortBase+maxContainers` missing from `run.ports` or declared without `httpSupport: true`, `maxContainers` the pool's own from the direct service list, at most 10 — the stanza's 3 when it reads none); for a dev serving several parts, `service` (the declaration names no server whose `service` is the part's pool, names a server with no `service` — every part's leases would run it — or one whose `service` is no part's pool: a pool container's supervisor runs the entries naming its pool and those naming none, PM-12), and for a single part `service` names each server whose `service` is neither the pool nor empty — the dev's own hostname, which a variant-B dev writes on every entry, among them: no lease of the pool would run it. `image.findings` speak the dependency rule (§8 DP; the dev-pool atom) and never hold the deploy: `deps-lockfile` (nothing in `build.addToRunPrepare`) and `deps-baked` (no `run.prepareCommands` item besides the zcp-devsup line names `/var/www`; the message adds that what `build.deployFiles` ships into `/var/www` is wiped by every reset when the setup ships any, and the declaration's `prepare` when it has one). With nothing missing, `next` is `zerops_deploy sourceService=<dev> targetService=<pool> setup=<pool> sha=<HEAD commit>` — a cross-deploy from a commit (§4.9, GF-3/GF-4), never the dev's dirty tree; zcp writes and commits nothing, the agent commits and deploys. The step is done (`image.done`) when nothing is missing and the pool, by the direct service list, runs an app version with code — not the startWithoutCode placeholder — created at or after that committer date (`image.deployed` `{created, configCommittedAt, current}`): the platform's app-version reads name no commit (§12.7 GF-10), so the time is the evidence, never a proof (GF-5); a later commit that changes the config file makes the step `image` again, one that changes other files does not. The step is then `move`, `lease` or `ready-to-retire` (§13.1, PM-6). A live process of the pool that is a build (`stack.build`, labelled build or deploy, `ops.ProjectActivity`) makes the step `image` with `zerops_process action="wait" service=<pool>` and reads no commit; any other live process of the pool keeps the provision step's wait. `TestPoolMigrateImage_SetupChecks_ReadySetup_NothingMissing`, `TestPoolMigrateImage_SetupChecks_EachGapNamed`, `TestPoolMigrateImage_SetupChecks_DependencyRule_FindingsNeverHoldTheDeploy`, `TestPoolMigrateImage_SetupChecks_PreviewPortsFollowMaxContainers`, `TestPoolMigrateImage_Done_PoolRunsAnImageNewerThanTheSetup`, `TestPoolMigrateImage_Uncommitted_Named`, `TestReadPoolMigrateImage_ReadsTheCommitNotTheTree`, `TestReadPoolMigrateImage_ZeropsYmlFallbackAndNoDeclaration`, `TestReadPoolMigrateImage_UnknownCommit_Missing`, `TestReadPoolMigrateImage_NoCommitYet_NoSSH`, `TestReadPoolMigrateImage_WritesNothing`, `TestReadPoolMigrateImage_SSHFailure_KeepsTheSSHError`, `TestReadPoolMigrateImage_ScriptRunsOnlyReadOnlyGit`, `TestParsePoolMigrateImageRead_CutShortOrFailed_Error`, `TestPoolMigrateImageStep_ReadySetup_NamesTheShaDeploy`, `TestPoolMigrateImageStep_SetupNotReady_NamesNoDeploy`, `TestPoolMigrateImageStep_DependencyFindings_DeployStillNamed`, `TestPoolMigrateImageStep_PoolRunsTheCommitsSetup_StepIsMove`, `TestPoolMigrateImageStep_PoolRunsAnOlderImage_NamesTheDeployAgain`, `TestPoolMigrateImageStep_BuildInFlight_Waits`, `TestPoolMigrateImageStep_CommitUnreadable_NamesNoDeploy`, `TestPoolMigrateImageStep_ReadSideWritesNothing`, `TestPoolMigrateImageStep_EnvAction_LandsOnTheDeploy`, `TestPoolMigrate_StepFromLiveState_EnvDone_Image`, `TestPoolMigrateImage_SetupChecks_SeveralParts_DeclarationNamesEachPartsPool`. |
| PM-12 | Several parts. A dev serving several parts — a pair with more than one stage, the monorepo model: one repository, one dev service running every part's dev servers — moves each part onto a pool of its own: the part of a stage is that stage and its setup, its pool the stage hostname without its `stage` suffix then `pool` (`webstage` → `webpool`), never an input — a hostname the platform refuses is `pool-hostname-invalid`, one a service that is no pool holds or two parts would share is `pool-hostname-taken`, each naming the part. Every answer lists `parts` `[{stage, setup, pool, step}]` and walks one part (`part`, its stage; `pool` and `inputs.pool` its pool): the part a call names — by its stage as `service`, or by its pool as `pool` (one naming no part, or another part than `service`, is `pool-not-a-part`) — else the first part not yet `ready-to-retire`. Each part walks the single part's steps on its own pool with the dev's one census and work's branch: its own stanza, its pool's env compared with its own stage only (PM-4), its pool setup checked in the dev's commit with the declaration's service keys (PM-11), its lease. A part `ready-to-retire` while another is not names the flow call `service=<dev> pool=<that part's pool>` of the first part that is not; once every part is `ready-to-retire` the answer names no part (no `pool`, `part`, `env` or `image`) and its next call is the retire `service=<dev>`. The retire waits for every part: each part's step (`not-ready`, naming every part short of it with its step) and each part's lease, held and verified (`no-lease`, `lease-unverified`, naming the part's pool), are read on every call that could start or resume it, before the split — a resume at `rewritten` whose re-key never began among them (PM-7); the dev's own guards, census, fence, bundle and fingerprint are one, and its delete one. The record names every part (`parts` `[{stage, pool, poolServiceId, poolSetup}]`, in the order of the pair's stages; `pool` empty); the pair locks held from the fence through the delete are the dev's and every part's pool's. `rewritten` is `workflow.MigratePairToPools`, inside one `.services.lock`: one pool-keyed pair per part — the dev's pair re-keyed onto the part's pool as PM-10 keys a single part's, holding only the part's stage and that stage's records, its `devPool.migratedFrom.parts` naming every part's pool — written, then the dev-keyed file removed; it refuses a stage no part names (it would stay under the dead dev's key), a part naming no stage of the pair and a pool another pair keys or names; a part already written by a cut split keeps what its file holds, and a split done is a no-op. An abort after it is `workflow.RevertPoolPairs`: the parts' pairs merge back into the dev's, as their files hold them now — the stages in the parts' order, each stage's records from its part's, the pair-wide facts from the first part's, the dev's own back from `migratedFrom` — or, the split cut before the dev's file went, the parts' copies are removed; `RevertPoolPair` refuses one part of a split. A Mate's split pairs share one HQ repository and its one change; each pool's credential is renewed on its own pair. The group recipe carries the dev a split came from once, with every part's stage in the split's order. Once the declaration names the parts' pools, the dev service's own supervisor (`hostname` the dev's) runs none of those servers after its next reload, start or restart — they run on the parts' leases. `TestPoolMigrate_MultiPart_OnePoolPerPart_WalksTheFirstUnfinishedPart`, `TestPoolMigrate_MultiPart_PartNamedByStageOrPool`, `TestPoolMigrate_MultiPart_PoolHostnameRefused`, `TestPoolMigrate_MultiPart_ReadyPartPointsAtTheNext`, `TestPoolMigrate_MultiPart_EveryPartReady_NamesTheRetire`, `TestPoolMigrate_MultiPart_ImageChecksTheDeclarationsServiceKeys`, `TestPoolMigrateEnv_MultiPart_WritesOnlyTheNamedPartsPool`, `TestPoolMigrateRetire_MultiPart_WaitsForEveryPart`, `TestPoolMigrateRetire_MultiPart_SplitsThePairOnePoolPerPart`, `TestPoolMigrateRetire_MultiPart_ResumeTable`, `TestPoolMigrateRetire_MultiPart_ResumeWaitsForEveryPart`, `TestPoolMigrateRetire_MultiPart_CutSplitFinishes`, `TestPoolMigrateRetire_MultiPart_AbortAfterTheSplit_MergesBack`, `TestMigratePairToPools_SplitsThePairOnePerPart`, `TestMigratePairToPools_NotEveryPartOnItsPool_Refused`, `TestMigratePairToPools_Resume`, `TestMigratePairToPool_MultiPartPair_Refused`, `TestRevertPoolPairs_MergesWhatTheFilesHoldNow`, `TestRevertPoolPairs_CutSplit_DropsThePartsCopies`, `TestRevertPoolPair_SplitPair_Refused`, `TestGroupRecipeInputs_SplitPoolPairs_CarryOneDevServiceWithEveryPart`, `TestPoolMigrate_Integration_MultiPart_OnePoolPerPart`, `TestPoolMigrate_Integration_MultiPartRetireSplitsThePair`. |
| PM-13 | Variant B. zcp sets up no new code volume (docs/spec-mate.md §2.8a.4a), so every variant-B pair this flow moves was recorded before, and its deploy, dev-server and status results name this flow; the flow itself is unchanged. A pair whose repository lives on a code volume (docs/spec-mate.md §2.8a.4a: `ServiceMeta.CodeVolume` `{hostname, mountPath, devServices}`, a `local-storage` service each dev service of the pair mounts through `run.volume` in zerops.yaml, applied by a deploy — no import stanza mounts it) migrates like a dev serving several parts (PM-12), one pool per part. Its repository is the volume's checkout: the census (PM-2), the image step's read of the commit (PM-11), the bundle and the fingerprint (PM-3, PM-5) read it through the dev's mount at `mountPath` — the volume is a filesystem of its own, so the bundle's mirror in `/tmp` copies the checkout's objects and hard-links none (a hard link across the two fails; live-verified 2026-10-24, on the dev's mount and in the volume's own container at `/data` alike) — and crew worktrees are looked for under `/var/www/.crew/` and under `.crew/` of that mount. Each part's pool takes the env (PM-4) and the scale (its stanza) of the dev service the part runs on: the pair's dev service named like the part's stage (`apistage` → `apidev` or `api`), as the stand-up pairs them, else the dev half — every key but `GIT_TOKEN`: the pair is one repository, whose one GIT_TOKEN git-push-setup writes on the dev half it pushes from, so every part's pool is offered, and on the person's yes copied, the dev half's (PM-4). The retire's guards (PM-6) also read every further dev service by id — a live one is recorded with its id, one gone or absent from the direct list is skipped, one DELETING refuses `dev-deleting` — and the volume by id (`volume-not-live` when absent, gone or unreadable); a live process on any of them is `dev-busy`, an env reference to any is `env-reference`, a domain routed to any dev service is `domain`; and the checkout's config file (zerops.yaml, else zerops.yml, its working tree read over SSH, `ops.CodeVolumeMountingSetups`) naming the volume in `run.volume` of a stage's setup or a part's pool setup refuses `volume-mounted`, naming each — one `mounts` the volume, several `mount` it: the platform refuses to delete a volume any service still mounts, and the volume goes last, once no dev service is left to change the checkout through. `wouldDestroy.targets` — and the acknowledgment's `acknowledgedTargets` — are the dev, each further dev service and the volume, in that order, each with the id it is deleted by; `notKept` names the dev's container files outside the volume's mount — the path its bundle reads, `/var/www` itself among them — and adds each further dev service's URLs and container files and the volume itself (a volume created later under its hostname is new and empty). The record keeps `devServices` `[{hostname, serviceId}]` and `codeVolume` `{hostname, serviceId, mountPath, fingerprint, bundle, restore, refused}`. The phases: `fenced` takes every dev service's mount on this container down; `bundled` also reads the volume's own fingerprint of the checkout in its container at `/data` (`ops.CodeVolumeDataRoot` — the volume service's own mount of `/var/www` shows none of it), never compared with a dev service's reading, whose paths differ, and one it cannot read aborts; `rewritten` carries `codeVolume` onto the pool-keyed pair (PM-10); `deleting` re-checks the dev's fingerprint while no dev service is deleted yet — a change still aborts cleanly — then deletes the dev and each further dev service by its recorded id, one at a time, each awaited until it reads gone by id (PM-6), one already gone never deleted again. Deleting a dev service keeps the volume and its files, and the other dev services keep their mount; the volume's delete is the one step that loses the checkout. Once every dev service reads gone the pair names none of them (`workflow.SettleMigratedCodeVolume`), the dev's HQ backoff record goes, and the phase is `deleting-volume`: the checkout is read again at `/data` — one that moved since the bundle holds the delete (`fingerprint-changed`, the record kept) until a call carries `force=true`, after the person agreed, which first takes a second verified bundle from the volume itself (`<dev>.volume.bundle`, `codeVolume.bundle`; the newer copy, holding the late writes, so the retired answer names it as the one to restore from, with `codeVolume.restore`, and the first bundle as the older) and reads the checkout again after it, since that bundle's fingerprint was read before it streamed: one that moved while it streamed and was verified holds the delete again (`fingerprint-changed`, the record kept) — then the volume is deleted by its recorded id and its delete's process is read: finished, and the volume gone by id, clears `codeVolume` from the pair and the retire is `retired`; failed with the platform's asynchronous refusal — the delete answers a process that fails with no `failReason` and the error `containerAction` "This Local Storage volume is still mounted by other services…", which names no service, the volume then reading FAILED and still working — keeps `codeVolume` and the record at `deleting-volume`, records `codeVolume.refused`, and answers `retiring` with the refusal `volume-mounted`: some service still mounts the volume and the platform does not say which; it names the stages and pools whose setup in the checkout mounts it when the volume's container can read that, and the way out (remove `run.volume` from the setup and deploy — a deploy replaces the container without the mount — or delete the service), then the same acknowledged call. The resume rows (PM-7) of a retire whose dev is gone — deleted by the retire, or outside it: the further dev services and the volume decide from their own states by id — anything DELETING keeps the record (call again); everything gone finishes, `codeVolume` cleared; whatever is left is deleted only on the acknowledged call, the pair re-keyed first and, the dev gone before the bundle, the repository bundled from the volume at `/data`, whose census holds an unforced retire as the dev's does; a dev service still live is deleted before the volume whatever phase the record reached. At `deleting-volume` the volume live or FAILED is deleted again by its id after the `/data` re-read, DELETING keeps the record, gone finishes. `zerops_delete` refuses a further dev service or the volume of a retire in flight, pointing at the dev's retire, and the read side answers `retiring` named by either. A deploy to the pool fills no code volume (the fill after a dev half's deploy asks `DevIsPool`). ## Appendix A: Recovery Patterns

| Symptom | Cause | Fix |
|---------|-------|-----|
| Build FAILED: "command not found" | Wrong buildCommands | Check runtime knowledge |
| Build FAILED: "module not found" | Missing deps | Add to buildCommands |
| App crash: "EADDRINUSE" | Port conflict | Match port to zerops.yaml |
| App crash: "connection refused" | Wrong env var | Check envVariables vs discovered |
| HTTP 502 | Subdomain not active (auto-enable skipped on managed/prod, or failed) | Redeploy (auto-enable retries), or `zerops_subdomain action="enable"` as explicit recovery |
| Empty response | Not on 0.0.0.0 | Fix binding |
| READY_TO_DEPLOY after deploy | Start failed | Check start command, runtime version |
